## 1. WHAT THIS ROUND DOES (the design pass 1 converged on; nine seats + the Fable adjudication)

`dev_footbridge.c` (DC21285 "footbridge", CATS machine) ended the emulator process with `exit(1)` when a guest accessed
five interrupt-controller registers in the direction the datasheet does not define: writes to IRQ_STATUS +0x180,
IRQ_RAW_STATUS +0x184, FIQ_STATUS +0x280, FIQ_RAW_STATUS +0x284 (`:6803`: "The Status and RawStatus are read-only, and
EnableClear and Soft are write-only"), and a read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR +0x18c (Table 7-10's read column at 18Ch is `--`).
The fix is option-set member 3 -- DECLINE (no state change; a declined read returns 0 as the fork's POLICY, the nearest
datasheet support being `:1652` "nonexistent CSR ... zeros are read" and Figure 7-2 `:6814`) + a per-site latched
`fatal()` announce carrying `(once)`; five per-site `int` latch fields (fable5 Q3; the file had no latch of any kind); the
+0x18c arm drops its `odata = d->irq_enable` leak (that value is 188h's answer, not 18Ch's).  Four live `exit(1)` remain
in the file: the two SDRAM reads (+0x100/+0x104, `fbsdrampair`) and two construction-time ones in DEVINIT.  NOT touched,
by ruling: +0x28c (FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read) already declines silently -- `fiqenableread` facet (2), with the Opus seat's
dissent ("fix it in the same commit") recorded verbatim there; 190h/290h (IRQSoft/FIQSoft) reach `default:` --
`fbirqsoftunimpl`.

In the SAME commit the three probes' kill control (a +0x18c read that relied on the process dying) is RETIRED and
replaced by two HARNESS LEGS through each probe's own launcher: **H1** (an unknown `-E` name: NO prompt AND the
diagnostic `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` naming THIS run's input AND `WIFEXITED && == 1`) and **H2** (`quit`
after a prologue VERIFIED by a fresh `reg` -- r3 bit 0 clear, r5 = 0x11223344, r6 = 0x1011, the timer-mask value
0x00345678 in r1 (sites) / r7 (drain, witness) -- then ONE more ordinary `reg` that must fail with `dead` set BEFORE
teardown, and a reap of exit 0).  Both non-obvious H2 clauses are load-bearing: a child dead BEFORE the prologue shows
`next send fails` and `dead` IDENTICALLY (measured, pass 1).

The detector: (a) `footbridge_sites_probe.py` arm S -- the five sites in ONE process, each accessed twice with a legal
sibling read between (rows S1-S5 values, SF1/SF2 final raw reads, N0 five distinct once-announces, N1-N5 per-site
access-local text counts, controls SC1/SA1); (b) `diff_footbridge.c` P-rows (rung-1 DETECTOR, never a witness): a
calloc'd instance seeded BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE (irq_status 0x18, irq_enable 0x30, fiq_status 0x50, fiq_enable 0xc0 -- so
masked != raw), each declined access at three widths, preservation, the read EXPRESSIONS, the announce count (fatal() is a
counter there), a byte-FIRST fresh instance (P9); (c) H1/H2 in all three launchers; (d) gate_arm.sh pins on the legs'
DISCRIMINATING tokens and the arm-S rows.  Two clause-(e) disclosures stand: no GUEST row observes a nonzero status
(irq_status stays 0 under single-step even with a timer armed -- host timers stop at the prompt), and the FIQ read
expressions are pinned only against seeded values no product code produces (`fiq_status` has no writer in the tree).

## 2. SETTLED PREMISES, RE-INLINED SO NOBODY SPENDS AN ANSWER RE-DERIVING THEM (scope: this round)

* `fatal()` PRINTS and returns; it does not exit and is not gated by `-q` (`src/core/debugmsg.c:384-391`; `debug()` is
  gated at `:375`).  Every process death in this device is an explicit `exit(1)`.
* The five sites and directions are TRUE 5/5 (Table 7-10 `dc21285.txt:6821-6833`; A7.3.29-7.3.33; the one-sentence
  form `:6803`).  Table 7-1's "R = Read only. Writes have no effect." is A7.1's PCI-CONFIGURATION legend (`:4505-4520`,
  extracted staggered) -- it TRANSFERS to the CSR tables but is not a sentence about these registers.
* FAULT (member 5) is NOT available on ARM: `memory_rw.c:603-625` dispatches a device-access exception for MIPS and M88K
  only (no `MEM_ARM` arm).  `dev_unreadable.c`'s `return 0` yields MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED with no abort delivered.
* The file had NO latch field (15 fields, `:60-85`); `struct footbridge_data` is zeroed by DEVINIT's memset (`:771`) AND
  by the offline differentials' `calloc` (`regress/diff_footbridge.c:151`, `diff_fbpending_bound.c:143`).
* The differential's instance has NULL `d->irq` interrupt callbacks under calloc; the enable arms call
  INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq) -- so P-rows seed by direct field write and never through +0x188/+0x18c writes (the P-rows
  install counting no-ops on `d->irq` too, so a legal EnableClear write (P8) is safe).
* Five DIFFERENT startup refusals (`-E nosuch`, no file, missing file, bad option, `-M notanumber`) ALL exit 1 with no
  prompt (Opus, pass 1, s10_legmut.out); only the diagnostic text naming the input discriminates H1.  Two of them print
  the banner first, so the text search covers the whole buffer.
* Unknown `-E`: `machine.c:240` `fatal("\nSorry, emulation \"%s\"", stype)` ... `fatal(" is unknown.\n")`, returns 0 at
  `:258`; `main.c:543-544` `if (!res) exit(1);` -- before any prompt.  `quit`: `debugger_cmds.c:1636-1637` sets the
  shutdown flags; `main.c:807-821` cleans up and returns 0.
* The pass-1 packet's per-site row design was BELOW THE FLOOR: a two-site shared latch and an aggregate latch each swept
  it 29/29 because every row ran one site in its own process (Opus, s6_table.out); the one-process arm S with the N0
  count row is the measured repair (5 / 4 / 1).  A constant-0 IRQ_STATUS read (`m_swallow`) and a dropped `&` at FIQ_STATUS
  (`m_fiqraw`) also swept the guest rows -- the offline P-rows are the repair for the first and a NARROWING for the second.
* The FIQ enable is unobservable from the guest by any route today (+0x288 reads 0: `fiqenableread` facet 1).
* Rows named A1/A2/D1/K1 already existed on the sites probe and are gate-pinned (K1 is RETIRED by this diff; its old
  gate check would read 0, which is why the gate edit is forced).

## 3. THE DIFF -- six files, 34 hunks, against SEC HEAD f685d75 (frozen copies under `_scratchpad/fbd5/p2/`; md5 of the
concatenation 600f9c4b).  THE PRODUCT CHANGE IS ONE FILE and it is inlined WHOLE, as the entire enclosing function:

 		}

 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)

== SIBLING PARITY (the arms the diff de-fatalises vs their nearest siblings) ==
  IRQ_STATUS/IRQ_RAW_STATUS/FIQ_STATUS/FIQ_RAW_STATUS write arms  vs  the READ arms of the same cases: the read arms
    have a VALUE channel (masked / raw); the write arms after #545 have a DECLINE channel (announce count, state
    unchanged).  Rows: S1/S2/S4/S5 (value, between declines), N1/N2/N4/N5 (count), SF1/SF2 (final raw), P-rows (seeded).
  IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read arm  vs  IRQ_ENABLE_SET read arm (#539 accepted it, E0-E6): SET returns the value, CLEAR
    declines with 0 -- asymmetry BY DESIGN (Table 7-10 names no read register at 18Ch); rows N3 (both reads 0, count 1),
    S3 (enable intact), E5 (the legal Clear write still works), P3/P8.
  IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read  vs  FIQ_ENABLE_CLEAR read (+0x28c): the twin ALREADY declines silently; NOT touched -- no
    witness (0 before and after), fiqenableread facet (2) carries the Opus dissent verbatim.  OWED SENTENCE, in the
    probe header: written.
  IRQSoft/FIQSoft (190h/290h) reach default: and announce every access: fbirqsoftunimpl, not this round.

== GUARD FIELD vs BODY FIELD ==
  Guard operands (new): warned_irq_status_w, warned_irq_raw_w, warned_irq_clear_r, warned_fiq_status_w,
    warned_fiq_raw_w -- each written at exactly ONE site (its own decline), read at exactly one site.  grep -c
    'warned_.*= 1' == 5 after the patch; no other writer.  A guard testing the WRONG field (copy-paste) = mutant M4.
  Body fields: none (the decline body does nothing but latch + announce); the legal-direction bodies read
    irq_status/irq_enable/fiq_status/fiq_enable -- pinned by P0/P7 seeded reads and S1-S5.
  Disagreeing-state row owed: irq_status != 0 with a decline in flight -> P-rows (seeded 0x10), since no guest row
    can reach it (Q2 f, disclosed for GUEST rows).

## 6. QUESTIONS (answer all; number them)

Q1 THE PREMISE AUDIT.  Every load-bearing premise in sections 1-2, stated as a question: TRUE / FALSE / NOT CHECKABLE,
   with the anchor that settles it.  In particular: does the fixed function's common tail still run for every declined
   access (odata 0 for the read, `return 1`)?  Does any of the five arms change state?  Is the +0x18c read's leak gone?
Q2 THE SMALLEST EDIT THAT BREAKS THIS AND STILL PASSES.  Against the diff as written and the table as measured: name the
   edit, the row you expect to stay green, and why.  Candidates the table did NOT build: a `&& len == 4` conjunct on
   ANOTHER site than IRQ_STATUS (P9 covers only IRQ_STATUS); a latch cleared by a LEGAL read of a sibling register; a
   decline that stores into the OTHER family's status; an announce text that names the right register but drops
   `(once)`; a gate pin regex that a duplicated green line satisfies.
Q3 THE FLOOR.  Walk clauses (a)-(f) against the SHIPPED composite detector: (a) fails on the pre-fix build, (b) fails
   on a verbatim reinstatement at EVERY site (the table reinstated ONE), (c) fails on a null medium, (d) DEPENDS-ON
   closure -- enumerate the predicates dominating the edited lines (the five latch tests, `writeflag`) and the
   operand-swap forms drawn from the bodies' read fields; (e) is the contract's disclosure list complete and honest;
   (f) is any row relative-only (anchored?).  A breach is L2 -- say so plainly.
Q4 THE HARNESS LEGS.  Do H1/H2 as implemented in the THREE launchers establish what the kill arm established?  Read
   each launcher's H2 code path: is `dead` observed BEFORE teardown in all three?  Is the prologue verification scored
   (drain/witness: `bad` empty, `len(ex) == PROLOGUE_N`, four register values)?  Does the sites probe's `send()` now
   forward its timeout, and does anything else in the tree depend on the old form?  Name the smallest edit to a leg that
   leaves it green while proving nothing (the table measured "omit quit" and "any nonzero exit"; what else?).
Q5 OPTION-SET AND RECORDS.  Is member 3 still the right disposition given what pass 2 sees?  Is anything in the
   comments or the probe header a WRONG RECORD (a claim the diff or the table refutes)?  Row counts: the differential's
   IDENTITY pin is 111 -- enumerate the P-rows to check it.
STANDING LICENCE: if, while verifying a claim, you found something ELSE -- say so.

End with a seat record: model, files read (with their md5 if you can compute one), nothing run / what you ran.


## OPERATIONAL RULE FOR EVERY SEAT (appended by panel.sh, not by the author)

**Do not run `run.sh`, any `gate_*.sh`, or the battery.** Every measurement you need is
inlined above. A gate takes minutes -- longer than your response budget -- and a second
concurrent invocation corrupts the operator run in progress, because the gates share one
log directory. A seat that runs a gate produces no review AND may invalidate someone
else s. Building and running a single detector or mutant in your own temp directory is
fine and encouraged; driving the harness is not.

If you want a measurement that is not inlined, NAME IT and say why. Naming it is worth
far more to this panel than a timeout.

-exit 1 and say so.  If both survived, the guest instructions are not reaching the device at
-all and every SURVIVED verdict above is worthless; if both died, the probe is killing the

+THE HARNESS LEGS (#545), which replaced the kill bridge.  Until #545 this probe's failability
+control was arm K: arm L plus ONE guest read of IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR (+0x18c), which the device
+answered with fatal() then exit(1).  #545 declines that read (and the four undefined-direction
+writes) instead of dying, so nothing a guest can do to this device ends the process any more
+-- except the two SDRAM reads at +0x100/+0x104 (`fbsdrampair`), which no arm here touches.  The
+control that "this harness notices a dead child" therefore has to come from the HARNESS, and
+it comes from two legs that need no product defect:
+  H1 STARTUP REJECTION: launch with an unknown `-E` name.  machine.c prints
+     `Sorry, emulation "<name>" is unknown.` and main.c exits 1 before any prompt.  The row
+     requires NO prompt AND that diagnostic naming THIS run's input AND `WIFEXITED && == 1`.
+     The text clause does all the work: five different startup refusals (bad option, bad -M,
+     missing file, ...) ALL exit 1 with no prompt, so "exit != 0" or "exit == 1" alone proves
+     nothing (measured, pass 1).
+  H2 QUIT AFTER A VERIFIED PROLOGUE: arm L's program, stepped to its end, a FRESH `reg` proving
+     r3 bit 0 clear, r5 = 0x11223344, r6 = 0x1011 and r1 = 0x00345678 (the prologue really
+     ran), then `quit`, then ONE MORE ordinary `reg`: it must fail and `dead` must be set
+     BEFORE teardown, and the reap must be `WIFEXITED && == 0`.  Both non-obvious clauses are
+     load-bearing: a child that died BEFORE the prologue finished produces `next send fails`
+     and `dead` IDENTICALLY, and is separated only by exit == 0 and the fresh-reg values --
+     a K2 without them goes green exactly when a product fix has FAILED (measured, pass 1).
+  Negative controls (PRETABLE, not shipped): omit the `quit` -> the next `reg` succeeds and H2
+  reds; move the teardown ahead of the observation -> H2 must red (it does not observe anything).
+
+ARM S (#545) -- the five declined sites in ONE PROCESS.  Each site is accessed TWICE in its
+undefined direction with a LEGAL read of a sibling register between the two, and every access
+must (i) leave the process alive, (ii) leave all five interrupt registers unchanged, and (iii)
+announce EXACTLY ONCE across the whole arm, in the site's own text.  ONE process is the whole
+point: a latch shared between two sites, or one aggregate latch, is INVISIBLE to any design
+that runs one site per process -- measured in pass 1, two total escapes of a per-site design.
+  enable 0x30 -> +0x188 (IRQ), 0xc0 -> +0x288 (FIQ); decline writes carry 0x10 (IRQ, inside
+  0x30) and 0x40 (FIQ, inside 0xc0), so a store into the status field would show in BOTH the
+  raw and the masked read.
+  N1 str 0x10 -> +0x180 x2   (legal read between: +0x184 -> r7, row S2)
+  N2 str 0x10 -> +0x184 x2   (legal read between: +0x180 -> sl, row S1)
+  N3 ldr +0x18c x2 -> r9, r8 (both 0: the IRQEnable value that used to be computed there
+                              is 188h's answer; legal read between: +0x188 -> fp, row S3 = 0x30)
+  N4 str 0x40 -> +0x280 x2   (legal read between: +0x280 -> r2, row S4)
+  N5 str 0x40 -> +0x284 x2   (legal read between: +0x284 -> lr, row S5)
+  SF1/SF2 FINAL raw reads +0x184 -> r1, +0x284 -> sp, after every decline (a store landed by
+     the LAST write of any pair shows here; r1 is A2's register in arm L and is free here).
+  N0 the five distinct `(once)` texts each appear exactly once in the arm's diagnostics
+     (5 on the fix; 4 under a two-site shared latch; 1 under one aggregate latch -- measured).
+  N1..N5 per-site: the site's own text appears exactly once (ACCESS-LOCAL text, so a misnamed
+     register reds its own row, not a total).
+  Widths (a byte or halfword decline) are NOT rowed here: a `len == 4` conjunct on the decline
+  cannot be seen by a count that another access of the same site already latched.  They are

FILE GXEMUL-SEC/regress/footbridge_sites_probe.py
1: #!/usr/bin/env python3
2: """#432: the DC21285 "footbridge" driven by REAL GUEST INSTRUCTIONS, with no rig.
3: 
4: WHY THIS EXISTS ALONGSIDE regress/diff_footbridge.c.  The offline differential calls
5: footbridge_effective_cycles() and dev_footbridge_access() directly, so it proves BEHAVIOUR
6: but cannot prove the device is REACHABLE by a guest -- it would stay green under a change
7: that unmapped it (a memory_device_register length, a DM_ flag, or machine_cats.c's
8: `addr=0x42000000` line).  Until this probe, R6 was THE ONLY CODE ROUND IN THIS TREE WITH NO
9: REACHABILITY WITNESS OF ANY KIND: no rig boots a footbridge and there is no m8online analog.
10: This is that analog.  It goes through the real address decode and the real memory_rw
11: plumbing, on a COLD DEBUGGER -- no boot, no disk image, no guest OS.
12: 
13: *** THE TRAP THIS PROBE EXISTS TO SURVIVE, and it is the whole reason for arm T. ***
14: machine_cats.c:131 calls arm_setup_initial_translation_table(), which (cpu_arm.c:239-255)
15: writes a section descriptor for i in 0..255 at VA (j<<28)+(i<<20) -> PA i<<20 -- i.e. the
16: TOP NIBBLE OF THE VA IS IGNORED and only the low 256 MB of physical space is mapped
17: anywhere.  The footbridge sits at PA 0x42000000, outside that window, so under the boot-time
18: MMU it is UNREACHABLE: VA 0x42000000 aliases to PA 0x02000000 and a load returns 0.
19: 
20: That is not a hypothetical.  The first version of this probe returned 0x0 on every device
21: row WITH ITS RAM CONTROL STILL GREEN, and 0x0 is exactly what "the device is not there"
22: looks like -- so a survival-shaped or absence-shaped assertion would have CONFIRMED THE
23: OPPOSITE CONCLUSION.  A RAM liveness row cannot see it, because VA 0x9000 maps to PA 0x9000
24: identically with the MMU on or off.
25: 
26: So the guard is not a comment, it is arm T: THE SAME PROGRAM WITH THE THREE MMU-DISABLE
27: INSTRUCTIONS REPLACED BY NOPS MUST NOT PRODUCE THE DEVICE'S ANSWER.  If arm T ever reads
28: 0x1011, the discriminator is dead and every other row here is meaningless -- and it goes RED
29: rather than silently green.  Arm L additionally reads CP15 c1 BACK after the mcr and asserts
30: bit 0 is clear, so the precondition is MEASURED rather than inferred from the store having
31: been issued.  Three separate statements, deliberately: the guest turned the MMU off, the two
32: arms really differ in MMU state, and only the MMU-off arm can see the device.
33: 
34: VALUES, NOT SURVIVAL (rounds 79/80, and m8820x_sites_probe.py's header says the same).
35: A row that only asks "did the host stay up" cannot tell a reachable device from an absent
36: one -- an unmapped read survives too, it just returns 0.  So the two device rows assert
37: values that ONLY dev_footbridge.c can produce:
38:   * A1 reads VENDOR_ID and requires 0x1011 (dev_footbridge.c `case VENDOR_ID`, the
39:     DC21285_VENDOR_ID assignment).
40:     Nonzero and specific: the hand-assembled-encoding trap this project has been bitten by
41:     yields 0 from a wrong register field, and 0 is the one answer this row rejects.
42:   * A2 writes 0x12345678 to TIMER_1_LOAD and reads back 0x00345678.  THE VANISHING TOP BYTE
43:     IS THE HANDLER'S OWN SIGNATURE: `case TIMER_1_LOAD`'s write branch (one case serves all four
44:     timers through the fold) stores `idata & TIMER_MAX_VAL`
45:     (0x00FFFFFF, dc21285reg.h:371).  RAM, or any decode that merely absorbed the store,
46:     would return 0x12345678 unchanged.  This row therefore distinguishes "the device
47:     handled it" from "the write landed somewhere", which A1 alone cannot.
48: 
49: THE HARNESS LEGS (#545), which replaced the kill bridge.  Until #545 this probe's failability
275: def run_arm(label, prog, emul="cats", quit_leg=False):
276:     """Plant `prog`, step it one instruction at a time, report registers and liveness.
277:     #545: `emul` is the -E name (leg H1 passes an unknown one and expects NO prompt); `quit_leg`
278:     runs leg H2 after the register read -- quit, then one more ordinary interaction, observed
279:     BEFORE teardown."""
280:     pid, fd = pty.fork()
281:     if pid == 0:
282:         os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", emul, "-M", "32",
283:                         "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
284:         os._exit(127)
285:     buf = ""
286:     dead = [False]
287: 
288:     def rd(t=0.4):
289:         nonlocal buf
290:         r, _, _ = select.select([fd], [], [], t)
291:         if fd not in r:
292:             return True
293:         try:
294:             d = os.read(fd, 65536)
295:         except OSError:
296:             dead[0] = True
297:             return False
298:         if not d:
299:             dead[0] = True
300:             return False
301:         buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
302:         return True
303: 
304:     #  Signature order is not free choice: gate_hygiene.sh pins the CANONICAL #392 call form
305:     #  as a literal `return wait(mark=_mark, echo=<x> if <x> else None)`, tightened three
306:     #  times because every looser prefix was a substring of the broken form too.  Written any
307:     #  other way, a probe is simply not covered by that ratchet -- which is worse than failing
308:     #  it.  So timeout goes last, and send() ends on the pinned form.
309:     def wait(mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
310:         t = time.time()
311:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
312:             if not rd():
313:                 return False
314:             resp = buf[mark:]
315:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
316:                 continue
317:             if len(buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
318:                 return True
319:         return False
320: 
321:     def send(x, timeout=60):
322:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
323:         _mark = len(buf)
324:         n = 0
325:         try:
326:             while n < len(b):
327:                 n += os.write(fd, b[n:])
328:         except OSError:
329:             dead[0] = True
330:             return False
331:         return wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
332: 
333:     status, regs, badis = "?", {}, []
334:     h2 = {}                        # #545 leg H2 observations, all taken BEFORE teardown
335:     #  KEYWORD, and this is not style.  A positional wait(120) silently becomes mark=120 under
336:     #  the pinned parameter order -- a real behaviour change, and the exact mistake the
337:     #  m8820x probe's conversion had to fix in the same commit.
338:     if not wait(timeout=120):
339:         status = "NO-PROMPT"
340:     else:
341:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA, 0x11223344))
342:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 4, 0x12345678))
343:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 8, P))
344:         send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + 12, Q))
345:         for off, word, _t, _c in prog:

347:         send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)
348:         status = "SURVIVED"

350:             mark = len(buf)

FILE GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_drain_probe.py
280: # ------------------------------------------------------------------ session
281: class Session:
282:     def __init__(self, emul="cats"):           # #545: leg H1 passes an unknown name
283:         self.buf, self.dead = "", False
284:         self.emul = emul
285:         self.pid, self.fd = pty.fork()
286:         if self.pid == 0:
287:             os.execvp(BIN, [BIN, "-V", "-T", "-E", emul, "-M", "32",
288:                             "0x%x:%s" % (CODE, STUB)])
289:             os._exit(127)
290: 
291:     def rd(self, t=0.4):
292:         r, _, _ = select.select([self.fd], [], [], t)
293:         if self.fd not in r:
294:             return True
295:         try:
296:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
297:         except OSError:
298:             self.dead = True
299:             return False
300:         if not d:
301:             self.dead = True
302:             return False
303:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
304:         return True
305: 
306:     #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
307:     #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
308:     #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
309:     #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
310:     #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
311:     #
312:     #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
313:     #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
314:     #
315:     #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
316:     #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
317:     #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
318:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
319:         t = time.time()
320:         seen = len(self.buf)
321:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
322:             if not self.rd():
323:                 return False
324:             if len(self.buf) != seen:
325:                 seen = len(self.buf)
326:                 t = time.time()
327:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
328:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
329:                 continue
330:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
331:                 return True
332:         return False
333: 
334:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
335:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
336:         #  `_mark`, not `mark`: gate 6's fresh-mark census keys on that exact spelling.
337:         #  Every other pty probe in the tree writes it that way, so a probe spelling it
338:         #  `mark` has the guard PRESENT and UNCOUNTED -- two of the three counts move and
339:         #  the third does not, a signature that reads like a defect and is not one.
340:         _mark, n = len(self.buf), 0
341:         try:
342:             while n < len(b):
343:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
344:         except OSError:
345:             self.dead = True
346:             return False
347:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
348: 
349:     def free_run(self, seconds):
350:         mark = len(self.buf)
351:         b, n = b"continue\n", 0
352:         try:
353:             while n < len(b):
354:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
355:         except OSError:
356:             self.dead = True
357:             return False, 0.0
358:         t0 = time.time()
359:         while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
360:             if not self.rd(0.25):
361:                 return False, time.time() - t0
362:         try:
363:             os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
364:         except OSError:
365:             self.dead = True
366:             return False, time.time() - t0
367:         ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
368:         t1 = time.time()
369:         if not ok and not self.dead:
370:             try:
371:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
372:             except OSError:
373:                 pass
374:             ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
375:             t1 = time.time()
376:         return ok, t1 - t0
377: 
378:     def read_words(self, addr, n):
379:         mark = len(self.buf)
380:         if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
381:             return None
382:         out = {}
383:         for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
384:             m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
385:             if not m:
386:                 continue
387:             base = int(m.group(1), 16)
388:             for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
389:                 out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", int(w, 16)))[0]
390:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
391:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
392: 
393:     def regs(self, which=(3, 5, 6, 7)):
394:         mark = len(self.buf)
395:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
396:             return {}
397:         seg, got = self.buf[mark:], {}
398:         for k in which:
399:             m = re.search(r"\br%d\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % k, seg)
400:             if m:
401:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
402:         return got
403: 
404:     def close(self, patience=20.0):
405:         try:
406:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
407:         except OSError:
408:             pass
409:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
410:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
411:         eof = False
412:         while True:
413:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
414:             if left <= 0:
415:                 break
416:             if not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
417:                 eof = True
418:                 break
419:         try:
420:             os.close(self.fd)
421:         except OSError:

423:         #  #539 (pass 2): BOUNDED reap.  A child that survives the master close (SIGHUP ignored or
424:         #  blocked, or stopped) would hang a blocking waitpid forever; poll, then kill, then wait.
425:         pid, st, gone = 0, None, False
426:         t1 = time.time()
427:         while pid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
428:             try:
429:                 pid, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, os.WNOHANG)

FILE GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_witness.py
230:             d = os.read(self.fd, 65536)
231:         except OSError:
232:             self.dead = True
233:             return False
234:         if not d:
235:             self.dead = True
236:             return False
237:         self.buf += d.decode("latin1", "replace")
238:         return True
239: 
240:     #  #467: an INACTIVITY deadline, not a total-time one.  This used to read
241:     #  `t = time.time()` once and loop `while time.time() - t < timeout`, so a boot
242:     #  that normally takes 40 s and took 90 s on a LOADED HOST failed -- while bytes
243:     #  were arriving the whole time.  Slow was indistinguishable from stuck, and the
244:     #  battery could only be trusted on an idle machine.
245:     #
246:     #  `rd()` cannot report progress: it returns True both when data arrives and when
247:     #  its select() times out quietly.  So progress is `len(self.buf)` changing.
248:     #
249:     #  STRICTLY MORE PERMISSIVE for a run that produces output, IDENTICAL for one that
250:     #  does not -- it cannot turn a passing run into a failing one.  `timeout` now
251:     #  means SILENCE, which is the thing actually worth failing on.
252:     def wait(self, mark=0, echo=None, timeout=60):
253:         t = time.time()
254:         seen = len(self.buf)
255:         while time.time() - t < timeout:
256:             if not self.rd():
257:                 return False
258:             if len(self.buf) != seen:
259:                 seen = len(self.buf)
260:                 t = time.time()
261:             resp = self.buf[mark:]
262:             if echo is not None and echo not in resp:
263:                 continue
264:             if len(self.buf) > mark and resp.rstrip().endswith("GXemul>"):
265:                 return True
266:         return False
267: 
268:     def send(self, x, timeout=60):
269:         b = (x + "\n").encode("latin1")
270:         _mark = len(self.buf)
271:         n = 0
272:         try:
273:             while n < len(b):
274:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
275:         except OSError:
276:             self.dead = True
277:             return False
278:         return self.wait(mark=_mark, echo=x if x else None, timeout=timeout)
279: 
280:     def free_run(self, seconds):
281:         """`continue`, drain for `seconds`, then SIGINT back to the prompt.
282: 
283:         Returns (ok, measured_free_run_seconds).  The pty is DRAINED throughout: a full
284:         pty buffer blocks the emulator, which would look like a device that stopped
285:         ticking -- the exact conclusion this probe is trying to reach, so it must not be
286:         manufacturable by the harness."""
287:         mark = len(self.buf)
288:         b, n = b"continue\n", 0
289:         try:
290:             while n < len(b):
291:                 n += os.write(self.fd, b[n:])
292:         except OSError:
293:             self.dead = True
294:             return False, 0.0
295:         t0 = time.time()
296:         while time.time() - t0 < seconds:
297:             if not self.rd(0.25):
298:                 return False, time.time() - t0
299:         try:
300:             os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
301:         except OSError:
302:             self.dead = True
303:             return False, time.time() - t0
304:         ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
305:         t1 = time.time()
306:         if not ok and not self.dead:
307:             try:
308:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGINT)
309:             except OSError:
310:                 pass
311:             ok = self.wait(mark=mark, timeout=20)
312:             t1 = time.time()
313:         return ok, t1 - t0
314: 
315:     def read_words(self, addr, n):
316:         """Read n consecutive words out of RAM.  `dump` prints the raw little-endian
317:         bytes, so a guest word 0x11223344 comes back as `44332211` and MUST be swapped."""
318:         mark = len(self.buf)
319:         if not self.send("dump 0x%08x 0x%08x" % (addr, addr + 4 * n)):
320:             return None
321:         out = {}
322:         for line in self.buf[mark:].splitlines():
323:             m = re.match(r"\s*0x([0-9a-f]{8})\s+((?:[0-9a-f]{8}\s+){1,4})", line)
324:             if not m:
325:                 continue
326:             base = int(m.group(1), 16)
327:             for i, w in enumerate(m.group(2).split()):
328:                 raw = int(w, 16)
329:                 out[base + 4 * i] = struct.unpack("<I", struct.pack(">I", raw))[0]
330:         vals = [out.get(addr + 4 * i) for i in range(n)]
331:         return None if any(v is None for v in vals) else vals
332: 
333:     def regs(self, which=(0, 1, 3, 5, 6, 7, 8, 9)):
334:         mark = len(self.buf)
335:         if not self.send("reg", timeout=30) or self.dead:
336:             return {}
337:         seg = self.buf[mark:]
338:         names = {10: "sl", 11: "fp", 12: "ip", 13: "sp", 14: "lr"}
339:         got = {}
340:         for k in which:
341:             nm = names.get(k, "r%d" % k)
342:             m = re.search(r"\b%s\s*=\s*0x([0-9a-fA-F]+)" % nm, seg)
343:             if m:
344:                 got[k] = int(m.group(1), 16)
345:         return got
346: 
347:     def close(self, patience=20.0):
348:         try:
349:             os.write(self.fd, b"quit\n")
350:         except OSError:
351:             pass
352:         # Drain through EOF; closing a live master can SIGHUP the child.
353:         deadline = time.monotonic() + patience
354:         eof = False
355:         while True:
356:             left = deadline - time.monotonic()
357:             if left <= 0:
358:                 break
359:             if not self.rd(min(0.1, left)):
360:                 eof = True
361:                 break
362:         try:
363:             os.close(self.fd)
364:         except OSError:
365:             pass
366:         #  #539 (pass 2): BOUNDED reap.  A child that survives the master close (SIGHUP ignored or
367:         #  blocked, or stopped) would hang a blocking waitpid forever; poll, then kill, then wait.
368:         pid, st, gone = 0, None, False
369:         t1 = time.time()
370:         while pid == 0 and time.time() - t1 < 10.0:
371:             try:
372:                 pid, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, os.WNOHANG)
373:             except OSError:
374:                 gone = True      # ECHILD: already reaped elsewhere -- its pid may be recycled; never kill it
375:                 break
376:             if pid == 0:
377:                 time.sleep(0.05)
378:         harness_killed = False
379:         if pid == 0 and not gone:
380:             #  F2 (#539): a kill WE fire is labelled, so a red from it is attributed to the harness --
381:             #  a healthy child SIGKILLed by a too-short budget would otherwise read like a product
382:             #  signal death (byte-identical to the y_sigkill mutant).  Budgets: 20 s drain + 10 s here.
383:             harness_killed = True
384:             try:
385:                 os.kill(self.pid, signal.SIGKILL)
386:             except OSError:
387:                 pass
388:             try:
389:                 _, st = os.waitpid(self.pid, 0)
390:             except OSError:
391:                 st = None
392:         code = os.WEXITSTATUS(st) if st is not None and os.WIFEXITED(st) else None
393:         sig = os.WTERMSIG(st) if st is not None and os.WIFSIGNALED(st) else None
394:         if harness_killed and sig == signal.SIGKILL:
395:             sig = "HARNESS-KILL(%s)" % sig     # our kill; a kill landing on a zombie reaps the
396:                                                 # product's own status and stays unlabelled
397:         elif not eof and sig is not None:
398:             sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close() HUP'd a live child
399:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)

356: /*
357:  *  debug() and fatal():
358:  *
359:  *  These are here because of all the legacy code that uses them. Basically,
360:  *  debug() is mapped to something that is only displayed with verbosity
361:  *  at the default level, and fatal() is used to always print stuff. With some
362:  *  hacks to make it work reasonable with -q and when executing normally or
363:  *  when executing single-stepping.
364:  */
365: void debug(const char *fmt, ...)
366: {
367: 	va_list argp;
368: 	bool ss = single_step || about_to_enter_single_step;
369: 	int v = verbose;
370: 	if (emul_executing)
371: 		v--;
372: 	if (ss)
373: 		v++;
374: 
375: 	if ((quiet_mode && !ss) || v < 0)
376: 		return;
377: 
378: 	va_start(argp, fmt);
379: 	va_debug(argp, fmt);
380: 	va_end(argp);
381: }
382: 
383: 
384: void fatal(const char *fmt, ...)
385: {
386: 	va_list argp;
387: 
388: 	va_start(argp, fmt);
389: 	va_debug(argp, fmt);
390: 	va_end(argp);
391: }

FILE GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c
55: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT	14
56: #define	DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH		0x400
57: 
58: #define	N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS		4
59: 
60: struct footbridge_data {
61: 	struct interrupt irq;
62: 
63: 	struct pci_data *pcibus;
64: 
65: 	int		console_handle;
66: 
67: 	uint32_t	timer_load[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
68: 	uint32_t	timer_value[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
69: 	uint32_t	timer_control[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
70: 
71: 	struct interrupt timer_irq[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
72: 	struct timer	*timer[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
73: 	int		pending_timer_interrupts[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
74: 	/*  #442: the per-timer bound on the counter above, one emulated second of
75: 	    that timer's delivered rate, recomputed by reload_timer_value().  */
76: 	int		pending_bound[N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS];
77: 
78: 	int		irq_asserted;
79: 
80: 	uint32_t	irq_status;
81: 	uint32_t	irq_enable;
82: 
83: 	uint32_t	fiq_status;
84: 	uint32_t	fiq_enable;
85: 
86: 	/*  #545: one warn-once latch per undefined-direction access that used
87: 	    to end the process (DC21285 7.3.29-7.3.33: "The Status and RawStatus
88: 	    are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only").  Zeroed by
89: 	    DEVINIT's memset and by the offline differentials' calloc.  */
90: 	int		warned_irq_status_w;
91: 	int		warned_irq_raw_w;
92: 	int		warned_irq_clear_r;
93: 	int		warned_fiq_status_w;
94: 	int		warned_fiq_raw_w;
95: };
96: 
97: 
98: /*
99:  *  #442: the counter above was incremented without a bound, so a guest that armed
100:  *  a timer and never acked overflowed it -- and on overflow the int goes negative,
101:  *  DEVICE_TICK's `> 0` is false, and THE EMULATED CLOCK DELIVERS NOTHING FOR 2^31
102:  *  FURTHER TICKS.  Reproduced at rung 3 on an unmodified -E cats: two MMIO writes,
103:  *  then 42.95 s to the wrap and a 40 s dead window, measured three times.
104:  *
105:  *  THE COMPARISON PRECEDES THE INCREMENT, and that is not a style choice.  An
106:  *  increment-then-check form -- which is what the one in-tree precedent for this
107:  *  (dev_luna88k.c:252) actually does -- reintroduces the UB it is meant to remove
108:  *  if the counter ever reaches INT_MAX, because the ++ has already executed by the
109:  *  time the check runs.  luna88k is safe only because its own reset keeps the
110:  *  counter far below INT_MAX; the FORM is still the wrong one to copy.
111:  *
112:  *  Reset to 1 rather than 0, so one interrupt is still owed -- coalescing the debt
113:  *  rather than discarding it.  MEASURED to matter: a reset-to-0 mutant survives the
374: 				    d->timer_control[i], 0);
375: 				INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->timer_irq[i]);
376: 			}
377: 		}
378: 	}
379: }
380: 
381: 
382: /*
383:  *  footbridge_interrupt_assert():
384:  */
385: void footbridge_interrupt_assert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
386: {
387: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
388: 	d->irq_status |= interrupt->line;
389: 
390: 	// Patch from NetBSD:
391: 	// "fix hang interrupt issue. Always assert the irq's even if we have
392: 	//  already asserted the global irq for the device. prevents hangs with
393: 	//  cats."
394: 	if ((d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) /* && !d->irq_asserted */) {
395: 		d->irq_asserted = 1;
396: 		INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq);
397: 	}
398: }
399: 
400: 
401: /*
402:  *  footbridge_interrupt_deassert():
403:  */
404: void footbridge_interrupt_deassert(struct interrupt *interrupt)
405: {
406: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) interrupt->extra;
407: 	d->irq_status &= ~interrupt->line;
408: 
409: 	if (!(d->irq_status & d->irq_enable) && d->irq_asserted) {
410: 		d->irq_asserted = 0;
411: 		INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->irq);
412: 	}
413: }
414: 
415: 
416: /*
417:  *  Reading the byte at 0x79000000 is a quicker way to figure out which ISA
418:  *  interrupt has occurred (and acknowledging it at the same time), than
419:  *  dealing with the legacy 0x20/0xa0 ISA ports.
420:  */
421: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_isa)
422: {
423: 	/*  struct footbridge_data *d = extra;  */
424: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
425: 	int x;
426: 
427: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
428: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
429: 		fatal("[ footbridge_isa: WARNING/TODO: write! idata = 0x%016" PRIx64" ]\n", idata);
430: 	}
431: 
432: 	x = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.last_int;
433: 	if (x < 8)
434: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic1->irq_base + x;
435: 	else
436: 		odata = cpu->machine->isa_pic_data.pic2->irq_base + x - 8;
437: 
438: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
439: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len, odata);
440: 
441: 	return 1;
442: }
443: 
444: 
445: /*
446:  *  Reset pin at ISA port 0x338, at least in the NetWinder:
447:  *
448:  *  TODO: NOT WORKING YET!
449:  */
450: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_reset)
451: {
452: 	uint64_t idata = 0;
453: 
454: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE) {
455: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
456: 		if (idata & 0x40) {
457: 			debug("[ footbridge_reset: GP16: Halting. ]\n");
458: 			/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) cpu->running=0 already halts cleanly;
459: 			    the col-0 exit(1) was a debug hack that kills the host.  */
460: 			cpu->running = 0;
461: 		}
462: 	}
463: 
464: 	return 1;
465: }

468: /*
469:  *  The Footbridge PCI configuration space is implemented as a direct memory
470:  *  space (i.e. not one port for addr and one port for data). This function
471:  *  translates that into bus_pci calls.
472:  */
473: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge_pci)
474: {
475: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
476: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
477: 	int bus, dev, func, reg;
478: 
479: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
480: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN);
481: 
482: 	/*  Decompose the (direct) address into its components:  */
483: 	bus_pci_decompose_1(relative_addr, &bus, &dev, &func, &reg);
484: 	bus_pci_setaddr(cpu, d->pcibus, bus, dev, func, reg);
485: 
486: 	if (bus == 255) {
487: 		fatal("[ footbridge DEBUG ERROR: bus 255 unlikely,"
488: 		    " pc (might not be updated) = 0x%08x ]\n", (int)cpu->pc);
489: 		/*  #220: (Codex/Fable) don't exit() the host on a guest PCI
490: 		    access to the (unlikely) bus 255; treat it as handled.  */
491: 		return 1;
492: 	}
493: 
494: 	debug("[ footbridge pci: %s bus %i, device %i, function %i, register "
495: 	    "%i ]\n", writeflag == MEM_READ? "read from" : "write to", bus,
496: 	    dev, func, reg);
497: 
498: 	bus_pci_data_access(cpu, d->pcibus, writeflag == MEM_READ?
499: 	    &odata : &idata, len, writeflag);
500: 
501: 	if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
502: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, data, len|MEM_PCI_LITTLE_ENDIAN, odata);
503: 
504: 	return 1;
505: }
506: 
507: 
508: DEVICE_ACCESS(footbridge)
509: {
510: 	struct footbridge_data *d = (struct footbridge_data *) extra;
511: 	uint64_t idata = 0, odata = 0;
512: 	int timer_nr = 0;
513: 
514: 	if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE)
515: 		idata = memory_readmax64(cpu, data, len);
516: 
517: 	if (relative_addr >= TIMER_1_LOAD && relative_addr <= TIMER_4_CLEAR) {
518: 		timer_nr = (relative_addr >> 5) & (N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS - 1);
519: 		relative_addr &= ~0x060;
520: 	}
521: 
522: 	switch (relative_addr) {
523: 
524: 	case VENDOR_ID:
525: 		odata = 0x1011;  /*  DC21285_VENDOR_ID  */
526: 		break;
527: 
528: 	case DEVICE_ID:
529: 		odata = 0x1065;  /*  DC21285_DEVICE_ID  */
530: 		break;
531: 
532: 	case 0x04:
533: 	case 0x0c:
534: 	case 0x10:
535: 	case 0x14:
536: 	case 0x18:
537: 		/*  TODO. Written to by Linux.  */
538: 		break;
539: 
540: 	case REVISION:
541: 		odata = 3;  /*  footbridge revision number  */
542: 		break;
543: 
544: 	case PCI_ADDRESS_EXTENSION:
545: 		/*  TODO: Written to by Linux.  */
546: 		if (writeflag == MEM_WRITE && idata != 0)
547: 			fatal("[ footbridge: TODO: write to PCI_ADDRESS_"
548: 			    "EXTENSION: 0x%llx ]\n", (long long)idata);
549: 		break;
550: 
551: 	case SA_CONTROL:
678: 	case TIMER_1_CONTROL:
679: 		if (writeflag == MEM_READ)
680: 			odata = d->timer_control[timer_nr];
681: 		else {
682: 			d->timer_control[timer_nr] = idata;
683: 			/*  #432: both prescaler bits set is TIMER_EXTERNAL
684: 			    (0x0C, dc21285reg.h:364) -- a LEGAL named encoding,
685: 			    and it used to reach fatal()+exit(1) right here, so a
686: 			    guest selecting the external clock source KILLED THE
687: 			    HOST.  Arguably likelier than the zero load, since it
688: 			    is a documented mode rather than a malformed value.
689: 			    The rate an external clock would actually give is
690: 			    UNKNOWN and is NOT invented: footbridge_effective_cycles()
691: 			    now gives the encoding a defined, survivable meaning
692: 			    (the unprescaled period), and the real semantics are
693: 			    filed for a round that has a datasheet.  */
694: 			if (idata & TIMER_ENABLE) {
695: 				reload_timer_value(cpu, d, timer_nr);
696: 			} else {
697: 				d->pending_timer_interrupts[timer_nr] = 0;
698: 			}
699: 			INTERRUPT_DEASSERT(d->timer_irq[timer_nr]);
790: 	CHECK_ALLOCATION(d = (struct footbridge_data *) malloc(sizeof(struct footbridge_data)));
791: 	memset(d, 0, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
792: 
793: 	/*
794: 	 *  #442: the memset leaves pending_bound[] at 0, and a bound of 0 would pin
795: 	 *  the counter at 1 rather than bounding it.  That is UNREACHABLE today --
796: 	 *  reload_timer_value() sets the bound, and it is also the only thing that
797: 	 *  ever creates a struct timer, so no callback can fire before a bound
798: 	 *  exists.  Initialised here anyway, because that is a two-step argument
799: 	 *  about a signal handler and a later edit adding a timer elsewhere would
800: 	 *  silently break it.
801: 	 *
802: 	 *  *** THE SEED IS 1, NOT THE CEILING, AND THE FIRST VERSION HAD THIS
803: 	 *  BACKWARDS. ***  It called the ceiling "the safe default: it can never
804: 	 *  throttle, only bound."  The ceiling is 68,157,440 -- at cats' 762.94 Hz
805: 	 *  that is TWENTY-FIVE HOURS of un-acked backlog, i.e. pre-fix behaviour
806: 	 *  for any run shorter than a day.  Measured: a mutant seeding exactly
807: 	 *  that value reads 3051 on the drain probe, indistinguishable from
808: 	 *  pre-fix.  If the scenario this seed exists for ever happened, it would
809: 	 *  hand that timer the LARGEST bound the fix permits.  1 can only
810: 	 *  under-deliver, which is the right direction for a value that should
811: 	 *  never be used.
812: 	 */
813: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++)
814: 		d->pending_bound[i] = 1;
815: 
816: 	/*  Connect to the CPU which this footbridge will interrupt:  */
817: 	INTERRUPT_CONNECT(devinit->interrupt_path, d->irq);
818: 
819: 	/*  DC21285 register access:  */
820: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, devinit->name,
821: 	    devinit->addr, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_LENGTH,
822: 	    dev_footbridge_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
823: 
824: 	/*  ISA interrupt status/acknowledgement:  */
825: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory, "footbridge_isa",
826: 	    0x79000000, 8, dev_footbridge_isa_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
827: 
828: 	/*  The "fcom" console:  */
829: 	d->console_handle = console_start_slave(devinit->machine, "fcom", 0);
830: 
831: 	/*  Register 32 footbridge interrupts:  */
832: 	snprintf(irq_path, sizeof(irq_path), "%s.footbridge",
833: 	    devinit->interrupt_path);
834: 	for (i=0; i<32; i++) {
835: 		struct interrupt interrupt_template;
836: 		char tmpstr[600];
837: 
838: 		memset(&interrupt_template, 0, sizeof(interrupt_template));
839: 		interrupt_template.line = (uint32_t)1 << i;
840: 		snprintf(tmpstr, sizeof(tmpstr), "%s.%i", irq_path, i);
841: 		interrupt_template.name = tmpstr;
842: 
843: 		interrupt_template.extra = d;
844: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_assert =
845: 		    footbridge_interrupt_assert;
846: 		interrupt_template.interrupt_deassert =
847: 		    footbridge_interrupt_deassert;
848: 		interrupt_handler_register(&interrupt_template);
849: 
850: 		/*  Connect locally to some interrupts:  */
851: 		if (i>=IRQ_TIMER_1 && i<=IRQ_TIMER_4)
852: 			INTERRUPT_CONNECT(tmpstr, d->timer_irq[i-IRQ_TIMER_1]);
853: 	}
854: 
855: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
856: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
857: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.10", irq_path);
858: 		break;
859: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
860: 		snprintf(irq_path_isa, sizeof(irq_path_isa), "%s.11", irq_path);
861: 		break;
862: 	default:fatal("footbridge unimpl machine type\n");
863: 		exit(1);
864: 	}
865: 
866: 	/*  A PCI bus:  */
867: 	d->pcibus = bus_pci_init(
868: 	    devinit->machine,
869: 	    irq_path,
870: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  PCI device io offset  */
871: 	    0x80000000,		/*  PCI device mem offset  */
872: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI port base  */
873: 	    0x00000000,		/*  PCI mem base  */
874: 	    irq_path,		/*  PCI irq base  */
875: 	    0x7c000000,		/*  ISA port base  */
876: 	    0x80000000,		/*  ISA mem base  */
877: 	    irq_path_isa);	/*  ISA port base  */
878: 
879: 	/*  ... with some default devices for known machine types:  */
880: 	switch (devinit->machine->machine_type) {
881: 	case MACHINE_CATS:
882: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
883: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 7, 0, "ali_m1543");
884: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
885: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 10, 0, "dec21143");
886: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
887: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 16, 0, "ali_m5229");
888: 		break;
889: 	case MACHINE_NETWINDER:
890: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
891: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 0, "symphony_83c553");
892: 		bus_pci_add(devinit->machine, d->pcibus,
893: 		    devinit->machine->memory, 0xc0, 11, 1, "symphony_82c105");
894: 		memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
895: 		    "footbridge_reset", 0x7c000338, 1,
896: 		    dev_footbridge_reset_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
897: 		break;
898: 	default:fatal("footbridge: unimplemented machine type.\n");
899: 		exit(1);
900: 	}
901: 
902: 	/*  PCI configuration space:  */
903: 	memory_device_register(devinit->machine->memory,
904: 	    "footbridge_pci", pci_addr, 0x1000000,
905: 	    dev_footbridge_pci_access, d, DM_DEFAULT, NULL);
906: 
907: 	/*  Timer ticks:  */
908: 	for (i=0; i<N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
909: 		d->timer_control[i] = TIMER_MODE_PERIODIC;
910: 		d->timer_load[i] = TIMER_MAX_VAL;
911: 	}
912: 
913: 	machine_add_tickfunction(devinit->machine,
914: 	    dev_footbridge_tick, d, DEV_FOOTBRIDGE_TICK_SHIFT);
915: 
916: 	devinit->return_ptr = d->pcibus;
917: 	return 1;
918: }

6931:            Interrupt Controller Registers
6932: 
6933:            There are 27 interrupt sources, and each interrupt source can be used to generate nIRQ or nFIQ (or
6934:            neither, or both). The control registers for the nFIQ and nIRQ control are identical. The interrupt
6935:            controller does not impose any priority on the interrupts; all enabled interrupts are delivered at
6936:            equal priority. The Status and RawStatus are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only.
6937:            Also, Enable and EnableSet share the same address for reads and writes respectively. Figure 7-2
6938:            shows the configuration for each interrupt source.

534: #  seat pointed out that the previous form still admitted

539: #  real substring of both the fixed and the broken code, which is exactly the
540: #  property a detector must not have.
541: #  *** EXTENDED 2026-08-20 FOR THE METHOD FORM, and narrowly on purpose given the history
542: #  in the paragraph above. ***  Every probe until now wrote `send()` as a closure over a local
543: #  `buf`, so the guard read `return wait(mark=_mark, ...)`.  fbpending_drain_probe.py is the
544: #  first CLASS-BASED probe in the tree: its guard is `return self.wait(mark=_mark, ...,

547: #

550: #  argument.  Both are anchored -- `mark=_mark` and the full `echo=` conditional still have to
551: #  be present and in order -- so neither re-opens the hole the three earlier tightenings closed:

72: uint64_t memory_readmax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len)
73: {
74: 	uint64_t x = 0; int i;
75: 	(void) cpu;
76: 	/*  Little-endian, which is what the rigs are; the device only ever reads back what
77: 	    this driver wrote, so the convention only has to be self-consistent.  */
78: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
79: 		x |= (uint64_t) buf[i] << (8*i);
80: 	return x;
81: }
82: void memory_writemax64(struct cpu *cpu, unsigned char *buf, int len, uint64_t data)
83: {
84: 	int i;
85: 	(void) cpu;
86: 	for (i = 0; i < len; i++)
87: 		buf[i] = (data >> (8*i)) & 255;
88: }
89: void console_putchar(int handle, int ch) { (void) handle; (void) ch; }
90: 
91: /*  The device asserts an interrupt on every delivered tick, and INTERRUPT_ASSERT calls
92:     through a function pointer in the struct.  A calloc'd struct leaves that NULL, so the
93:     first tick SIGSEGVs -- which looks exactly like the crash under test and would have made
94:     this whole file a liar.  Install counting no-ops instead.  */
95: static int irq_asserts, irq_deasserts;
96: static void noop_assert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_asserts++; }
97: static void noop_deassert(struct interrupt *i) { (void) i; irq_deasserts++; }
98: 
99: #include "../src/devices/dev_footbridge.c"
100: 
101: /*  Needs the struct, so it lives AFTER the device include.  */
102: static void arm_irqs(struct footbridge_data *d)
103: {
104: 	int i;
105: 	for (i = 0; i < N_FOOTBRIDGE_TIMERS; i++) {
106: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
107: 		d->timer_irq[i].interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
108: 	}
109: }
110: 
111: /* --------------------------------------------------------------- harness --- */
112: 
113: static int rows, fails;
114: static void check(const char *name, const char *got, const char *want)
115: {
116: 	rows++;
117: 	if (strcmp(got, want) == 0)
118: 		printf("  ok    %-54s %s\n", name, got);
119: 	else {
120: 		fails++;
121: 		printf("  FAIL  %-54s\n          got  %s\n          want %s\n",
122: 		    name, got, want);
123: 	}
124: }
125: static void check_u(const char *name, uint64_t got, uint64_t want)
126: {
127: 	char g[32], w[32];
128: 	snprintf(g, sizeof(g), "%" PRIu64, got);
129: 	snprintf(w, sizeof(w), "%" PRIu64, want);
130: 	check(name, g, w);
131: }
132: 
133: /*  The helper is static inside the shipped file, which is exactly why this driver includes
134:     the file rather than linking it.  */
135: int main(void)
136: {
137: 	struct footbridge_data *d;
138: 	struct cpu *cpu;
139: 	struct machine *machine;
140: 
141: 	/*  LINE-BUFFERED ON PURPOSE.  gate_offline.sh redirects this binary's stdout to a file,
142: 	    which makes it FULLY buffered -- so when a mutant re-introduces the crash, the log is
143: 	    0 BYTES and the operator gets a red row with nothing in it.  Measured: line-buffered,
144: 	    the same run leaves 943 bytes naming A1/B2/D1 as FAIL before it dies.  The gate's
145: 	    greps fail either way, so this is legibility rather than detection -- but it is this
146: 	    project's own recorded stdout-buffering trap, in a new place.  */
147: 	setvbuf(stdout, NULL, _IOLBF, 0);
148: 
149: 	cpu = calloc(1, sizeof(struct cpu));
150: 	machine = calloc(1, sizeof(struct machine));
151: 	d = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
152: 	cpu->machine = machine;
153: 	machine->emulated_hz = 100000000;	/*  100 MHz, an ordinary rate  */
154: 
155: 	{
156: 		/*
157: 		 *  SELFCHECK -- CAN THIS FILE'S COMPARATOR STILL FAIL?
158: 		 *
159: 		 *  Feed check() deliberate mismatches and require the failure counter to move,
160: 		 *  then un-record them so the rows are free.  If check() has been stubbed,
161: 		 *  neutered, or had its comparison edited away, EVERY other row in this file is
162: 		 *  silently inert and the gate cannot tell -- measured: 118 rows across five
163: 		 *  detectors stayed green under exactly that stub, row counts, identity rows and
164: 		 *  verdict tokens all intact.
165: 		 *
166: 		 *  TWO MISMATCHES, ONE ALPHABETIC AND ONE DIGIT-LEADING, and the second is not
167: 		 *  padding.  The first version fed only "a" vs "b", and a pass-2 seat defeated it
168: 		 *  with one line -- `isdigit(got[0]) || strcmp(...) == 0` -- because EVERY REAL ROW
169: 		 *  IN THIS FILE COMPARES NUMBERS.  118 rows went inert, this sentinel stayed blind,
170: 		 *  and four of the five gate-2 self-mutants stayed green too.  A sentinel whose
171: 		 *  input does not resemble the rows it vouches for is a sentinel with a whitelist.
172: 		 *
173: 		 *  THE HEALTHY PATH PRINTS AN ok-SHAPED LINE, DELIBERATELY.  The first version
610: 	}
611: 
612: 	/*
613: 	 *  #545 P-ROWS -- the five undefined-direction accesses that used to end the process are
614: 	 *  DECLINED with a latched announce.  These rows pin what no stepped guest can (the guest
615: 	 *  rows live in footbridge_sites_probe.py, arm S): PRESERVATION under a NONZERO status, the
616: 	 *  read EXPRESSIONS, the widths, and the latch -- fatal() is a counter here, so the announce
617: 	 *  is counted, never read (the misname check is the guest rows' job).
618: 	 *
619: 	 *  SEEDED BY DIRECT FIELD WRITE, NEVER THROUGH +0x188/+0x18c: this instance's d->irq
620: 	 *  callbacks are NULL (calloc), and the enable arms call INTERRUPT_ASSERT(d->irq).
621: 	 *  fiq_status has no writer in the product; seeding it pins the +0x280/+0x284 expressions
622: 	 *  against a value the product never produces -- a NARROWING of that gap, not its closure.
623: 	 */
624: 	{
625: 		struct footbridge_data *h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
626: 		unsigned char buf[8];
627: 		char nm[96], nm2[128];
628: 		int f0, i;
629: 		static const struct { uint64_t addr; int wf; int len; const char *what; } dec[] = {
630: 			{ IRQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P1 write IRQ_STATUS"       },
631: 			{ IRQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P2 write IRQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
632: 			{ IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, MEM_READ,  4, "P3 read IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR"  },
633: 			{ FIQ_STATUS,       MEM_WRITE, 4, "P4 write FIQ_STATUS"       },
634: 			{ FIQ_RAW_STATUS,   MEM_WRITE, 4, "P5 write FIQ_RAW_STATUS"   },
635: 		};
636: 		arm_irqs(h);
637: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
638: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
639: 		/*  seeds chosen so MASKED != RAW: a dropped `&` (raw returned as status) reds P0  */
640: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;	/*  masked 0x10, raw 0x18  */
641: 		h->fiq_status = 0x50;  h->fiq_enable = 0xc0;	/*  masked 0x40, raw 0x50  */
642: 		fatal_calls = 0;	/*  this block counts announces from a clean baseline  */
643: #define P_STATE_OK(tag) do { \
644: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_status, 0x18); \
645: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- irq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->irq_enable, 0x30); \
646: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_status preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_status, 0x50); \
647: 		snprintf(nm2, sizeof(nm2), "%s -- fiq_enable preserved", tag); check_u(nm2, h->fiq_enable, 0xc0); } while (0)
648: #define PN(suffix) (snprintf(nm, sizeof(nm), "%s: %s", dec[i].what, suffix), nm)
649: 		/*  the legal reads FIRST, on the seeded state: the expressions, not a constant 0  */
650: 		memset(buf, 0, sizeof(buf));
651: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
652: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_STATUS read = irq_status & irq_enable (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
653: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
654: 		check_u("P0 IRQ_RAW_STATUS read = irq_status (0x18)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x18);
655: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
656: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_STATUS read = fiq_status & fiq_enable (0x40)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x40);
657: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
658: 		check_u("P0 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read = fiq_status (0x50)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x50);
659: 		check_u("P0 the legal reads announce nothing", fatal_calls, 0);
660: 		for (i = 0; i < 5; i++) {
661: 			int a0 = irq_asserts, d0 = irq_deasserts, rv;
662: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
663: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);	/*  a value inside BOTH enables  */
664: 			rv = dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
665: 			check_u(PN("handler returned 1 (declined, not failed)"), rv, 1);
666: 			check_u(PN("announced exactly once"), fatal_calls - f0, 1);
667: 			if (dec[i].wf == MEM_READ)
668: 				check_u(PN("the declined read returns 0"), memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0);
669: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #1"));
670: 			check_u(PN("interrupt output untouched"), (irq_asserts - a0) + (irq_deasserts - d0), 0);
671: 			f0 = fatal_calls;
672: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
673: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, dec[i].len, dec[i].wf, h);
674: 			check_u(PN("#2 announced nothing more (latched)"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
675: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
676: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 1, dec[i].wf, h);
677: 			memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x70);
678: 			dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, dec[i].addr, buf, 2, dec[i].wf, h);
679: 			check_u(PN("byte and halfword accesses: still latched"), fatal_calls - f0, 0);
680: 			P_STATE_OK(PN("after #2, byte, halfword"));
681: 		}
682: 		/*  the latches are per SITE: five accesses, five announces, none shared  */
683: 		check_u("P6 five sites announced five times in one instance", fatal_calls, 5);
684: 		/*  a legal read after the declines still reads the seeded expressions  */
685: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
686: 		check_u("P7 IRQ_STATUS read after the declines (0x10)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x10);
687: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, FIQ_RAW_STATUS, buf, 4, MEM_READ, h);
688: 		check_u("P7 FIQ_RAW_STATUS read after the declines (0x50)", memory_readmax64(cpu, buf, 4), 0x50);
689: 		/*  a LEGAL EnableClear write still works, next to the declined read of the same address  */
690: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 4, 0x10);
691: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR, buf, 4, MEM_WRITE, h);
692: 		check_u("P8 legal EnableClear write: 0x30 & ~0x10", h->irq_enable, 0x20);
693: 		check_u("P8 ... announced nothing", fatal_calls, 5);
694: #undef PN
695: #undef P_STATE_OK
696: 		free(h);
697: 		/*  P9: a FRESH instance whose FIRST access to a site is a BYTE write.  A decline that fires only
698: 		    for len == 4 would let the byte fall through silently -- indistinguishable above, where the
699: 		    word access had already latched the site.  Here it must announce.  */
700: 		h = calloc(1, sizeof(struct footbridge_data));
701: 		arm_irqs(h);
702: 		h->irq.interrupt_assert = noop_assert;
703: 		h->irq.interrupt_deassert = noop_deassert;
704: 		h->irq_status = 0x18;  h->irq_enable = 0x30;
705: 		fatal_calls = 0;
706: 		memory_writemax64(cpu, buf, 1, 0x70);
707: 		dev_footbridge_access(cpu, NULL, IRQ_STATUS, buf, 1, MEM_WRITE, h);
708: 		check_u("P9 a BYTE write as the first access is declined AND announced", fatal_calls, 1);
709: 		check_u("P9 ... and stores nothing", h->irq_status, 0x18);
710: 		free(h);
711: 		fatal_calls = 0;
712: 	}
713: 
714: 	/*  IDENTITY, as every differential here carries.  #545: 33 -> 111 (78 P-rows).  */
715: 	{
716: 		char buf[32];
717: 		snprintf(buf, sizeof(buf), "%d", rows + 1);
718: 		check("IDENTITY row count -- guards against a stale copy", buf, "111");
719: 	}
720: 
721: 	printf("\n%d rows, %d failures\n", rows, fails);
722: 	printf("DIFF_FOOTBRIDGE_%s\n", fails == 0 ? "PASS" : "FAIL");
723: 	return fails ? 1 : 0;

438:             #  a healthy child SIGKILLed by a too-short budget would otherwise read like a product

455:             sig = "HARNESS-CLOSE(%s)" % sig   # the drain deadline expired; our close() HUP'd a live child
456:         return dict(exit=code, signal=sig)

459: def plant(s, prog):
460:     for off, v in CONSTS.items():
461:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, v))
462:     for off in OUTS:
463:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (DATA + off, POISON))
464:     for off, word, _t, _c in prog:
465:         s.send("put w 0x%08x, 0x%08x" % (CODE + off, word))
466:     s.send("pc=0x%08x" % CODE)

468: 
469: def spell_ok(expected, got):
470:     """The emulator's own spelling differs cosmetically from the generator's:
471:     it prints `ldr r5,[r4]` for a zero offset and `#0` rather than `#0x0`.  Those
472:     are FORMAT differences, not encoding differences -- the WORD is compared
473:     separately and exactly.  Normalise both sides so the check keeps its teeth
474:     (a wrong register or a wrong offset still fails) without red rows for
475:     punctuation."""
476:     def norm(t):
477:         t = re.sub(r"\s+", "", t.lower())
478:         t = re.sub(r",#0\]", "]", t)                      # [r4,#0]  -> [r4]
479:         t = re.sub(r"#0x([0-9a-f]+)",
480:                    lambda m: "#" + str(int(m.group(1), 16)), t)   # #0x100 -> #256
481:         return t
482:     return norm(expected) == norm(got)

485: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
486:     planted = {CODE + off: (w, t) for off, w, t, _ in prog}
487:     bad, covered, ex = [], set(), []
488:     for _ in range(n):
489:         mark = len(s.buf)
490:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
491:             bad.append("host died / no prompt at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
492:             break
493:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", s.buf[mark:])
494:         if not m:
495:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(ex) + 1))
496:             break
497:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
498:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
499:         ex.append((addr, word, got))

631:     dead_before_close = s.dead
632:     end = s.close()
633:     h2 = dict(status="SURVIVED" if prologue_ok else "PROLOGUE-FAILED", prologue_ok=prologue_ok,
634:               quit_rc=quit_rc, next_rc=next_rc, dead_before_close=dead_before_close, **end)

FILE GXEMUL-SEC/regress/fbpending_witness.py
411: 
412: def step_and_check(s, prog, n):
413:     """`step 1` n times; check every EXECUTED word against what was planted at that
414:     address AND its disassembly against the emulator's own spelling.  Returns
415:     (mismatches, covered_addresses, executed)."""
416:     planted = {CODE + off: (word, text) for off, word, text, _ in prog}
417:     bad, covered, executed = [], set(), []
418:     for _ in range(n):
419:         mark = len(s.buf)
420:         if not s.send("step 1", timeout=30) or s.dead:
421:             bad.append("host died or no prompt during step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
422:             break
423:         seg = s.buf[mark:]
424:         m = re.search(r"([0-9a-f]{8}):\s+([0-9a-f]{8})\s+([^\r\n<]+)", seg)
425:         if not m:
426:             bad.append("no step line at step %d" % (len(executed) + 1))
427:             break
428:         addr, word = int(m.group(1), 16), int(m.group(2), 16)
429:         got = re.sub(r"\s+", " ", m.group(3)).strip()
430:         executed.append((addr, word, got))
431:         if addr not in planted:
432:             bad.append("stepped 0x%08x, which was never planted" % addr)
433:             continue
434:         pw, pt = planted[addr]
435:         covered.add(addr)
436:         if word != pw:
437:             bad.append("0x%08x read back 0x%08x, planted 0x%08x" % (addr, word, pw))
438:         if pt is not None and got != pt:
439:             bad.append("0x%08x 0x%08x -> %r, expected %r" % (addr, word, got, pt))
440:     return bad, covered, executed

501:     s = Session()

503:         return dict(h1=h1, h2=dict(status="NO-PROMPT", **s.close()))
504:     plant(s, prog)

FILE _scratchpad/dc21285.txt
1630:        SA-110 Originated Transactions
1631: 
1632:        The following sections describe SA-110 originated bus transactions. These transactions are
1633:        classified by their address spaces (refer to Table 5-1).
1634: 
1635:        The 21285 controls the SA-110 clock, MCLK, by stretching the high time to stall the SA-110
1636:        during bus cycles (indicated by assertion of nMREQ by SA-110). The number of cycles that
1637:        MCLK stays high may be different for each address space and depends upon other 21285 activity
1638:        at the time nMREQ asserts.
1639: 
1640:        CSR Write
1641: 
1642:        The SA-110 write data is written to the selected CSR (the CSR with offset equal to SA-110 address
1643:        A[10:2]). The SA-110 is normally stalled for three cycles, but can be stalled longer. If a
1644:        nonexistent CSR is selected within the CSR address range, the write data is discarded (no error
1645:        action is taken).
1646: 
1647:        The CSR address space is listed in Tables 7-2, 7-3, and 7-4.
1648: 
1649:        CSR Read
1650: 
1651:        The selected CSR (the CSR with offset equal to SA-110 address A[10:2]) read data is driven onto
1652:        D[31:0]. If a nonexistent CSR is selected within the CSR address range, zeros are read (no error
1653:        action is taken). The SA-110 normally stalls for three cycles.
1654: 
1655: 21285 Core Logic for SA-110 Datasheet  3-1
1656: Transactions
1657: 
4503:            -- Miscellaneous registers
4504: 
4505: 7.1  PCI Configuration Space Registers
4506: 
4507:      PCI configuration space registers conform to the PCI Local Bus Specification, Revision 2.1. These
4508:      registers are accessed from the PCI by configuration reads and configuration writes, and are byte
4509:      writable. They are also accessible from the SA-110 (offset is from address 4200 0000h). Table 7-1
4510:      shows the allowable access to each register.
4511: 
4512: Table 7-1. Register Access             Definition
4513:                                        Read only. Writes have no effect.
4514:                    Abbreviation        Read/write.
4515:                    R                   Read. Write 1 to clear.
4516:                    R/W                 Write zero to clear.
4517:                    W1C                 Read. Write 1 to set.
4518:                    W0C                 Write only.
4519:                    W1S
4520:                    WO
4521: 
4522: 21285 Core Logic for SA-110 Datasheet                                     7-1
4523: Registers
6797: 
6798:            Interrupt Controller Registers
6799: 
6800:            There are 27 interrupt sources, and each interrupt source can be used to generate nIRQ or nFIQ (or
6801:            neither, or both). The control registers for the nFIQ and nIRQ control are identical. The interrupt
6802:            controller does not impose any priority on the interrupts; all enabled interrupts are delivered at
6803:            equal priority. The Status and RawStatus are read-only, and EnableClear and Soft are write-only.
6804:            Also, Enable and EnableSet share the same address for reads and writes respectively. Figure 7-2
6805:            shows the configuration for each interrupt source.

614: 			else

FILE regress/fbpending_witness.py
68:   E  encodings.  Byte-identical to A except ONE immediate: the sentinel is 0 instead of
69:      0x00AB0000, which makes the poll compare equal on the first pass and therefore

71:      hand-assembled words of the loop -- the words actually under test -- be checked

96:   a NONZERO seeded status -- something no stepped guest can reach here (Q2 f).
97:   S3 (a legal EnableClear write works, a zero write preserves) is E5's row already.
