99: ARM E -- #539's ACCEPT rows, in a FOURTH arm so that arms L/T/K are byte-identical to what
100: they were.  E carries its own C1/A1 (E0 = 0 is also what an ABSENT device returns, so the
101: E rows are meaningless unless this arm demonstrably reached the device) and then:
102:   E0 read IRQEnable before any write            -> 0            (reset value, 7.3.31)
103:   E1 write P=0x2002 to Set, read               -> P            (readable; catches a bit
104:                                                                   swap, a constant, a wrong
105:                                                                   field, a masked value)
106:   E2 write Q=0x60000000 to Set, read           -> P|Q          (pins |=; bit 30 set so a
107:                                                                   diagnostic kept for the top
108:                                                                   bits fires here)
109:   E3 ldrb  +0x188                              -> 0x02         (low byte: len 1 reaches the
110:   E6 ldrh  +0x188                              -> 0x2002        same case; the CSR drives
111:                                                                   D[31:0] and the SA-110 takes
112:                                                                   the lane, DC21285 3.2.x)
113:   E5 write P to Clear, read Set                -> Q            (Clear works; = the E2 value
114:                                                                   with P removed)
115:   E4 no BRACKETED diagnostic naming footbridge anywhere in arm E -- the `fatals` regex is
116:      `[ ... footbridge ... ]`, the house diagnostic shape; a kept fatal() with its exit gone
117:      is caught; a diagnostic rewritten WITHOUT brackets or without the device name is NOT
118:      (listed under NOT COVERED below)
119: Execution order is E0,E1,E2,E3,E6,E5: E6 wants 0x2002, which is true only while P is set.
120: P and Q are loaded from RAM (DATA+8, DATA+12), like 0x12345678: P is not a rotated ARM
121: immediate (bits 1 and 13); Q is (0x06 ROR 4) and is loaded the same way for uniformity.
122: Values land in r7,r1,r2,r9,fp,ip; r1/r2 are A2's registers in arm L and are free here
123: because arm E does not score A2.  TESTED PATTERNS, stated so the contract is checkable:
124: P = bits 1,13 and Q = bits 29,30 only; bit 31 and every other bit are untested by value.
125: 
126: CONTRACT (what this detector is designed to catch, and what it is not).  On the SET read
127: branch: the exit reinstated (E0 dies), reinstated only when irq_enable == 0 (E0), a wrong
128: field/constant/mask/bit-swap (E1, E2), a width guard (E3, E6), a kept diagnostic (E4), a
129: diagnostic kept for bit 30 (E4; E2 if it exits -- bit 31 is never set here, so a bit-31-only
130: guard is UNTESTED).  On the CLEAR arms: a broken `&= ~`
131: (E5).  On the FIVE DECLINED SITES (#545): the exit reinstated at any one site (that N row's
132: arm dies: N0 and every S row red), the announce unlatched (N-row count 2), a shared or
133: aggregate latch (N0 count 4 or 1), the +0x18c read leaking irq_enable (N3: 0x30 not 0), a
134: decline storing into a status field (S1/S2/S4/S5/SF1/SF2), a misnamed register (its N row),
135: a latch cleared by its own read (N3 count 2).  The harness legs H1/H2 as described above.
136: NOT COVERED by any GUEST row here: (f) a legal status read narrowed to a CONSTANT 0 -- irq_status
137: is 0 on every path a stepped guest can reach (the timer assert is host-timer driven and the
138: host timers are stopped at the prompt; measured with timer 1 armed), so S1/S2/S4/S5/SF1/SF2 pin
139: the VALUE 0 and catch a widening (`&` -> `|`, `odata = enable`), never a narrowing -- the offline
140: P-rows in diff_footbridge.c seed a nonzero status and pin that; the FIQ read EXPRESSION is
141: unpinned everywhere (`fiq_status` has no writer in the tree: `fiqenableread`).  +0x28c
142: (FIQEnableClear read, the FIQ twin of +0x18c) already declined SILENTLY before #545 and is
143: deliberately unchanged -- a read of it returns 0 before and after, so there is no witness;
144: it is `fiqenableread` facet (2), where the Opus seat's dissent (fix it in the same commit)
145: is recorded verbatim.  190h/290h (IRQSoft/FIQSoft) reach `default:` and announce on every
146: access: `fbirqsoftunimpl`.  (a) no row observes the
147: CPU IRQ line -- an edit to the write arm's assert condition (`irq_status | irq_enable`) is
148: invisible to every row here, because cpu_dyntrans.c suppresses interrupt delivery under
149: `step` and CPSR I/F stay set; it needs a free-running arm with a positive delivery control,
150: filed with IRQSoft; (b) +0x189..+0x18b: real hardware selects the same CSR by A[10:2] and
151: returns lanes 1-3, the model routes them to `default:` and returns 0 -- a device-wide decode
152: deviation, filed, not rowed; (c) irq_status and fiq_enable are 0 throughout, so a mutant
153: returning either at +0x188 is caught by E1/E2 only through the value, not distinguished
154: from a constant 0.  (d) D1 compares the disassembly of every STEPPED word; the landing pad is
155: planted and never stepped, and a step that ends the process is not compared.  (e) E4 sees only
156: a BRACKETED diagnostic that names footbridge (the `fatals` regex); a bare-text diagnostic on
157: the read path escapes it.
158: TEARDOWN CONVENTION this file's bounded drain rests on: rd() returns True on a select TIMEOUT
159: and False only on EOF/EIO, so the post-quit drain loop breaks on EOF or its deadline, never on
160: a quiet interval (the same convention as the two pending probes' Session.rd).
161: 
162: EVERY PLANTED WORD IS CHECKED AGAINST THE DISASSEMBLY THE EMULATOR PRINTS AS IT STEPS IT --
163: not against a separate `unassemble`, because the step line is the instruction that actually
164: executed.  This project has a recorded incident where a wrong register field made a gate row
165: measure the wrong thing for months.
166: 
167: COST, measured: see PROBE_WALL below -- 6 cold-debugger launches (#545: +S, +Q, +H), no boot.  A booting-rig row
168: was deliberately NOT written: no rig in this tree boots a footbridge machine at all, which is
169: the gap this probe closes, and adding one would put a load-sensitive wall-clock oracle into a
170: battery already carrying ~37.
171: 
172: usage: footbridge_sites_probe.py <gxemul-binary> <four-byte-raw-stub>

207:     emit(0x14, mov("r4", DATA), "scratch RAM")

209:     emit(0x1c, ldr("r6", "r0", 0), "A1 VENDOR_ID, expect 0x1011")

217:     emit(0x3c, ldr("r8", "r4", K_SEN), "sentinel 0x00FFFFFF")
218:     emit(0x40, ldr("ip", "r4", K_SPIN), "spin budget")
219:     emit(0x44, mov("sl", 0), "drained count")

227:     else:

NO-GO

The requested floor has an **L2 dependency-closure breach**: two wrong-family substitutions in the IRQ_STATUS read preserve every expected value in the frozen detector. Width and latch-reset coverage also have surviving edits.

This verdict applies to the brief’s frozen six-file patch and table 2. During review, the working differential and sites probe changed; those later changes are outside this verdict.

**1. Q1 — Premise audit**

| Question | Finding and anchor |
|---|---|
| Is HEAD really `f685d75`, with the six baseline files identical to `9c28ca5`? | **TRUE.** Verified with Git and byte comparisons between both revisions. |
| Is the frozen bundle six files and 34 hunks? | **TRUE.** Hunk counts: device 3, differential 1, gate 1, sites 14, drain 8, witness 7. Combined MD5 is `600f9c4becff14332f4547c08e1e9223`. |
| Are all five directions undefined by the register table? | **TRUE.** Enumerated below against [Table 7-10](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/dc21285.txt:6821). |
| Do all five declines reach the common tail and return 1? | **TRUE.** Each retains its `break`; the read reaches `memory_writemax64`, with initially zero `odata`, followed by `return 1`. [Device tail](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:776). |
| Does no state change? | **FALSE literally; TRUE for architectural device state.** The first access changes its warning latch. None of these bodies changes interrupt status, enables, timers, or interrupt outputs. Comments should say that explicitly. |
| Is the +0x18c enable-value leak gone, including subsequent reads? | **TRUE.** Neither the first nor subsequent declined read assigns `odata`; both reach the zero-writing tail. |
| Does `fatal()` print and return, independently of `-q`? | **TRUE.** [debugmsg.c:384](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384); the quiet-mode condition belongs to `debug()`. |
| Are exactly four live `exit(1)` calls left? | **TRUE.** Fixed-file lines 734, 759, 863, 899: two SDRAM reads and two DEVINIT branches. HEAD had nine live calls: five removed plus those four. |
| Would returning 0 deliver an ARM device-access abort? | **FALSE.** The failure-dispatch block contains MIPS and M88K exception delivery, followed by `MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED`; no ARM delivery arm. [memory_rw.c:603](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:603). |
| Did the original struct have 15 fields and no warning latch? | **TRUE.** Enumerated: irq, pcibus, console_handle, timer_load, timer_value, timer_control, timer_irq, timer, pending_timer_interrupts, pending_bound, irq_asserted, irq_status, irq_enable, fiq_status, fiq_enable. |
| Are the added latches zero-initialized? | **TRUE.** DEVINIT’s whole-struct `memset`; differential instances use `calloc`. |
| Are the P-instance IRQ callbacks NULL when accesses occur? | **FALSE.** They are initially NULL after allocation, but counting callbacks are installed before the accesses. Direct field seeding and the legal P8 write are therefore safe. [Frozen differential:636](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:636). |
| Are masked and raw seeds different? | **TRUE:** IRQ `0x18 & 0x30 = 0x10`; FIQ `0x50 & 0xc0 = 0x40`. **FALSE that this suffices to pin the expressions:** Q3 gives the collisions. |
| Does product code write `fiq_status`, or expose `fiq_enable` through another current route? | **No writer found; no observable current route.** Source-wide search found the declaration and reads only. +0x288 returns the default zero; masked FIQ status remains zero. |
| Are +0x28c and 0x190/0x290 outside this change as described? | **TRUE.** +0x28c reads silently reach the zero tail; Soft addresses reach `default:`. |
| Were the three executable kill arms retired and H1/H2 added? | **TRUE.** Their executable paths changed; their headers are inconsistent with that change—Q5. |
| Does unknown `-E` name the rejected input and exit 1? | **TRUE.** `machine.c:240–258`, `main.c:541–544`, and recorded H1 results agree. |
| Did all five other startup cases have no prompt and exit 1? | **TRUE in the supplied historical evidence.** `s10_legmut.out` separately records unknown emulation, no file, missing file, unknown option, and bad `-M`. |
| Can death before the prologue satisfy “next send fails” and `dead`? | **TRUE.** `s10_legmut.out` records precisely that, with failed fresh-register verification and exit 1. |
| Are the one-process count repair and previous escapes real? | **TRUE in the logs.** `s6_table.out`, `s8_all5.out`: isolated decline rows remain 20/20 under shared latches; combined counts enumerate 5, 4, 1. |
| Do the supplied stepped guest rows observe nonzero status? | **FALSE.** Their status observations are zero; `s7_cross.out` confirms the attempted timer case. Debugger stepping returns before restarting host timers. This establishes these programs’ limitation, not a universal impossibility for every conceivable stepped guest. |
| Are the original A1/A2/D1/K1 gate dependencies real? | **TRUE.** Present in the baseline; retaining K1 would reject the fixed device. |
| Are the panel unanimity, verbatim dissent, and original premeasurement chronology independently established? | **NOT CHECKABLE from the implementation and measurement evidence reviewed.** In particular, the current PRETABLE contains a post-table-1 amendment; its present mtime cannot establish when every sentence was first written. |

The five accesses enumerate as:

| Register | Offset, decimal | Declined direction |
|---|---:|---|
| IRQ_STATUS | `0x180`, 384 | Write |
| IRQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x184`, 388 | Write |
| IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR | `0x18c`, 396 | Read |
| FIQ_STATUS | `0x280`, 640 | Write |
| FIQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x284`, 644 | Write |

The read-only legend is indeed under PCI configuration §7.1. Applying its terminology elsewhere is an interpretation; Table 7-10 and the interrupt-controller text directly establish the directions. Zero for +0x18c remains a defensible fork policy, rather than an explicit register-specific datasheet promise.

**2. Q2 — Smallest edits that break behavior and survive**

The strongest survivor is a **single field substitution** in [IRQ_STATUS’s legal read](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:572):

- Replace the `irq_status` operand with `fiq_status`: `0x50 & 0x30 = 0x10`.
- Alternatively, replace the `irq_enable` operand with `fiq_status`: `0x18 & 0x50 = 0x10`.

Both leave **P0 and P7 green**. Guest IRQ/FIQ status fields are zero, so their observations remain zero too. Neither substitution affects preservation or announcement counts. **I predict the frozen 36/36 and 111/111 results remain green. These product mutants were not executed during this review.**

The requested candidates resolve as follows:

| Candidate | Result |
|---|---|
| Restrict another site’s announcement to `len == 4` | **Survives.** P2–P5 access the word first, then test already-latched byte/halfword accesses. P9 exercises only IRQ_STATUS. |
| Exclude halfword-first announcements at IRQ_STATUS | **Survives too.** P9’s fresh access is a byte, not a halfword. |
| Clear `warned_fiq_status_w` during a legal FIQ_RAW_STATUS read | **Survives.** In S, that read occurs after both FIQ_STATUS declines. P0 reads it before latching; P7 reads it after the decline tests. No later decline checks whether the latch was rearmed. |
| Store into the other family’s status | **Caught.** P_STATE_OK checks all four status/enable fields after each site; final guest raw reads also expose persistent nonzero corruption. |
| Keep the register name but remove `(once)` | **Caught.** Every N-row’s expected substring includes `(once)`; its count becomes zero and N0 fails. |
| Substitute a duplicate green line for a missing gate row | **Accepted by several pins.** I checked the regexes against modified copies of the recorded FIX log: replacing SA1 with SC1, S2 with S1, or SF2 with SF1 preserves the corresponding counts. N1–N5 have a distinct-ID check; these groups do not. This is a gate-check weakness, not a measured product-mutant escape. |

There is another width gap: [P3’s narrow reads](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:675) do not score their returned bytes or handler return values. The first word read returning zero does not establish those properties for byte and halfword reads.

**3. Q3 — The floor**

**(a) Pre-fix failure: satisfied.** Independently recounted the logs: 21 green and 15 red sites rows, totaling 36; the differential stops before producing P1’s result. The two intentional comparator-selfcheck failures printed in the differential logs are subsequently unrecorded and are not detector failures.

**(b) Verbatim reinstatement at every site: supported by control flow, incompletely measured.** S reaches its first declines at program offsets `0x40`, `0x4c`, `0x58`, `0x68`, `0x74`. Reinstating the corresponding terminating branch prevents the final register capture. The offline loop likewise visits P1 through P5. Table 2 measures only the FIQ_RAW_STATUS reinstatement; four isolated reinstatement measurements remain owed.

I decoded the S memory instructions. All have `cond=14, I=0, P=1, U=1, B=0, W=0, Rn=0`; their remaining fields are:

| Program offset(s) | Word | L | Rd | imm12 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 30 | `e5808188` | 0 | 8 | 392 |
| 38 | `e5809288` | 0 | 9 | 648 |
| 40, 48 | `e5802180` | 0 | 2 | 384 |
| 44 | `e5907184` | 1 | 7 | 388 |
| 4c, 54 | `e5802184` | 0 | 2 | 388 |
| 50 | `e590a180` | 1 | 10 | 384 |
| 58 | `e590918c` | 1 | 9 | 396 |
| 5c | `e590b188` | 1 | 11 | 392 |
| 60 | `e590818c` | 1 | 8 | 396 |
| 68, 70 | `e580c280` | 0 | 12 | 640 |
| 6c | `e5902280` | 1 | 2 | 640 |
| 74, 7c | `e580c284` | 0 | 12 | 644 |
| 78 | `e590e284` | 1 | 14 | 644 |
| 80 | `e5901184` | 1 | 1 | 388 |
| 84 | `e590d284` | 1 | 13 | 644 |

Thus the disputed access directions, offsets, widths, and destination registers agree with the actual words.

**(c) Null medium: supported by the predicates; not measured in table 2.** Missing/zero device responses fail the vendor and timer-mask anchors, S3, and announcement counts. A dead or empty launcher fails fresh-register/prologue checks. Several zero-valued rows individually remain green; the composite does not.

**(d) DEPENDS-ON closure: L2 BREACH.**

The edited bodies are dominated by address selection, direction selection, and these five predicates:

- `!warned_irq_status_w`
- `!warned_irq_raw_w`
- `!warned_irq_clear_r`
- `!warned_fiq_status_w`
- `!warned_fiq_raw_w`

For the four writes, the decline follows the false branch of `writeflag == MEM_READ`; for +0x18c it follows the false branch of `writeflag == MEM_WRITE`. The tail separately tests `writeflag == MEM_READ`.

The repeated accesses and five distinct announcements distinguish latch negation/removal, wrong latch tests, and shared latch assignments. They do not establish persistence after all legal sibling reads or first access at every width.

More decisively, enumerate the read-field substitutions with `I=0x18`, `E=0x30`, `F=0x50`, `G=0xc0`:

| Expression | Replace left operand | Replace right operand |
|---|---|---|
| IRQ masked `I&E`, expected `10` | `E&E=30`; **`F&E=10` survives**; `G&E=00` | `I&I=18`; **`I&F=10` survives**; `I&G=00` |
| FIQ masked `F&G`, expected `40` | `I&G=00`; `E&G=00`; `G&G=c0` | `F&I=10`; `F&E=10`; `F&F=50` |

Raw IRQ replacements yield `30/50/c0`, against `18`; raw FIQ replacements yield `18/30/c0`, against `50`. Those are distinguished. Commuting the original AND operands is equivalent and should not count as a kill obligation.

The seed choice therefore kills dropped masks while missing two wrong-field forms explicitly within this review’s closure requirement.

**(e) Disclosure: incomplete.** The guest/nonzero-status and synthetic-FIQ limitations are disclosed. First-width coverage, narrow-read results, latch rearming, and the two operand collisions are not adequately disclosed. “Widths … rowed OFFLINE” overstates what is checked. This contributes to the L2 finding.

**(f) Relative-only rows: no separate breach found.** State checks use literal seeds; P0 uses literal expected expressions; announcement deltas have initial and total-count anchors. C4’s inequality is paired with MMU-state and positive device controls. Relative comparisons exist, but the composite has independent absolute anchors.

**4. Q4 — Harness legs**

The implemented ordering is correct in all three frozen launchers:

| Launcher | Prologue verification | Observation before teardown |
|---|---|---|
| [Sites](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/footbridge_sites_probe.py:372) | Successful stepping, no disassembly mismatches, fresh r3/r5/r6/r1 | `quit`, `reg`, capture `dead`, then teardown |
| [Drain](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/fbpending_drain_probe.py:625) | `not bad`, exactly 21 executed entries, fresh r3/r5/r6/r7 | Lines 629–632 preserve that ordering |
| [Witness](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/fbpending_witness.py:505) | `not bad`, exactly 18 executed entries, fresh r3/r5/r6/r7 | Lines 509–512 preserve that ordering |

The counts enumerate `0x00,04,…,50` = 21 and `0x00,04,…,44` = 18. The register comparisons require the requested four values, and the scoring requires normal exit 0.

H1/H2 establish startup rejection and clean death after useful execution. **They do not reproduce the former control’s device-triggered, mid-session exit-1 channel.** The sites HEAD/M1 runs supply separate evidence for that channel; these pending-launcher legs do not.

Sites `send()` now forwards its timeout correctly. The actual [hygiene pattern](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh:553) accepts either a comma or closing parenthesis after the echo expression, so it recognizes both forms. No hygiene change is required; the probe’s claim that only the old literal form is recognized is stale.

A smaller vacuous H2 edit than moving teardown is to **reuse `quit_rc` as `next_rc` instead of sending the additional `reg`**. Recorded drain/witness FIX results already have `quit_rc=False`, `dead=True`, exit 0. H2 would stay green while no longer testing another ordinary interaction. This is a static prediction, not a newly executed mutant.

M13 also remains a valid concern: writing through a closed descriptor sets `dead` in the existing exception handler. “Before teardown by construction” describes today’s placement; it does not make a reordered harness self-detecting.

**5. Q5 — Option set and records**

**Member 3 remains appropriate.** The product branches preserve architectural state, provide the selected zero-read policy, retain the common tail, and bound announcements. The rejection concerns the detector floor and records.

The differential count is correct:

| Group | Rows |
|---|---:|
| P0 | 5 |
| P1 | 13 |
| P2 | 13 |
| P3 | 14 |
| P4 | 13 |
| P5 | 13 |
| P6 | 1 |
| P7 | 2 |
| P8 | 2 |
| P9 | 2 |
| **Added** | **78** |

Each write-site group contains return 1, first announcement, four preservation checks, interrupt-output check, repeated-announcement check, combined narrow-access announcement check, and four final preservation checks: **13**. P3 adds the first word-read value: **14**. Existing 33 + added 78 = **111**.

The sites rows also enumerate correctly: C1–C5 (5), A1/A2/A2b (3), I1 (1), H1/H2 (2), SC1/SA1 (2), S1–S5 (5), SF1/SF2 (2), N0–N5 (6), EC1/EA1 (2), E0–E6 (7), D1 (1): **36**.

These records need correction:

- Both pending-probe headers still describe K as a currently required terminating control.
- The sites header says FIQ expressions are “unpinned everywhere”; P0 and M8 contradict that wording. Say “not established through reachable nonzero guest state.”
- “fiq_enable … 0 throughout” is false across the new S arm, which writes `0xc0`.
- “S3 … a legal EnableClear write works, a zero write preserves” misidentifies S3. It reads IRQEnable; neither E5 nor P8 tests a zero Clear write.
- The differential comment says callbacks are NULL although they are installed before accesses.
- `0x70` is not “inside BOTH enables”: `70 & 30 = 30`, `70 & c0 = 40`. It has nonzero overlap with each.
- PRETABLE calls M13 measured; the final table explicitly says it was not built. The header’s “must red” assertion also contradicts PRETABLE’s explanation of why it greens.
- PRETABLE says SC1/SA1 cannot vary under listed mutants, but M1 makes both red.
- The working gate also contains an E-row uniqueness change absent from the frozen gate patch, beyond the separately disclosed unrelated tail.

One additional datasheet point: §7.3.30 says IRQRawStatus and FIQRawStatus always contain identical data. The `0x18/0x50` seeds violate that hardware invariant as well as current product reachability. They are legitimate synthetic detector inputs, but cannot establish hardware fidelity.

Before changing this verdict, add discriminating expression seeds, fresh byte/halfword cases for every site, returned-value/return-code checks for every declined read width, and a decline–legal-read–decline sequence covering latch persistence. Strengthen the missing distinct-ID gate pins and correct the records. Measure the additional mutants and the four remaining isolated reinstatements.

Seat record: **GPT-6 / Codex**. Read the entire brief, all six frozen diffs, relevant source and launcher paths, datasheet passages, PRETABLE/TABLE2, pass-1 `s6/s7/s8/s10` evidence, and parsed the recorded FIX/HEAD/mutant logs. Ran read-only Git, searches, hashing, in-memory patch comparisons, AST instruction decoding, arithmetic/row enumeration, and regex checks on log copies. **No emulator, compiler, gate, `run.sh`, or battery was run; no files were changed.**

The requested floor has an **L2 dependency-closure breach**: two wrong-family substitutions in the IRQ_STATUS read preserve every expected value in the frozen detector. Width and latch-reset coverage also have surviving edits.

This verdict applies to the brief’s frozen six-file patch and table 2. During review, the working differential and sites probe changed; those later changes are outside this verdict.

**1. Q1 — Premise audit**

| Question | Finding and anchor |
|---|---|
| Is HEAD really `f685d75`, with the six baseline files identical to `9c28ca5`? | **TRUE.** Verified with Git and byte comparisons between both revisions. |
| Is the frozen bundle six files and 34 hunks? | **TRUE.** Hunk counts: device 3, differential 1, gate 1, sites 14, drain 8, witness 7. Combined MD5 is `600f9c4becff14332f4547c08e1e9223`. |
| Are all five directions undefined by the register table? | **TRUE.** Enumerated below against [Table 7-10](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/_scratchpad/dc21285.txt:6821). |
| Do all five declines reach the common tail and return 1? | **TRUE.** Each retains its `break`; the read reaches `memory_writemax64`, with initially zero `odata`, followed by `return 1`. [Device tail](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:776). |
| Does no state change? | **FALSE literally; TRUE for architectural device state.** The first access changes its warning latch. None of these bodies changes interrupt status, enables, timers, or interrupt outputs. Comments should say that explicitly. |
| Is the +0x18c enable-value leak gone, including subsequent reads? | **TRUE.** Neither the first nor subsequent declined read assigns `odata`; both reach the zero-writing tail. |
| Does `fatal()` print and return, independently of `-q`? | **TRUE.** [debugmsg.c:384](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/core/debugmsg.c:384); the quiet-mode condition belongs to `debug()`. |
| Are exactly four live `exit(1)` calls left? | **TRUE.** Fixed-file lines 734, 759, 863, 899: two SDRAM reads and two DEVINIT branches. HEAD had nine live calls: five removed plus those four. |
| Would returning 0 deliver an ARM device-access abort? | **FALSE.** The failure-dispatch block contains MIPS and M88K exception delivery, followed by `MEMORY_ACCESS_FAILED`; no ARM delivery arm. [memory_rw.c:603](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/cpus/memory_rw.c:603). |
| Did the original struct have 15 fields and no warning latch? | **TRUE.** Enumerated: irq, pcibus, console_handle, timer_load, timer_value, timer_control, timer_irq, timer, pending_timer_interrupts, pending_bound, irq_asserted, irq_status, irq_enable, fiq_status, fiq_enable. |
| Are the added latches zero-initialized? | **TRUE.** DEVINIT’s whole-struct `memset`; differential instances use `calloc`. |
| Are the P-instance IRQ callbacks NULL when accesses occur? | **FALSE.** They are initially NULL after allocation, but counting callbacks are installed before the accesses. Direct field seeding and the legal P8 write are therefore safe. [Frozen differential:636](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:636). |
| Are masked and raw seeds different? | **TRUE:** IRQ `0x18 & 0x30 = 0x10`; FIQ `0x50 & 0xc0 = 0x40`. **FALSE that this suffices to pin the expressions:** Q3 gives the collisions. |
| Does product code write `fiq_status`, or expose `fiq_enable` through another current route? | **No writer found; no observable current route.** Source-wide search found the declaration and reads only. +0x288 returns the default zero; masked FIQ status remains zero. |
| Are +0x28c and 0x190/0x290 outside this change as described? | **TRUE.** +0x28c reads silently reach the zero tail; Soft addresses reach `default:`. |
| Were the three executable kill arms retired and H1/H2 added? | **TRUE.** Their executable paths changed; their headers are inconsistent with that change—Q5. |
| Does unknown `-E` name the rejected input and exit 1? | **TRUE.** `machine.c:240–258`, `main.c:541–544`, and recorded H1 results agree. |
| Did all five other startup cases have no prompt and exit 1? | **TRUE in the supplied historical evidence.** `s10_legmut.out` separately records unknown emulation, no file, missing file, unknown option, and bad `-M`. |
| Can death before the prologue satisfy “next send fails” and `dead`? | **TRUE.** `s10_legmut.out` records precisely that, with failed fresh-register verification and exit 1. |
| Are the one-process count repair and previous escapes real? | **TRUE in the logs.** `s6_table.out`, `s8_all5.out`: isolated decline rows remain 20/20 under shared latches; combined counts enumerate 5, 4, 1. |
| Do the supplied stepped guest rows observe nonzero status? | **FALSE.** Their status observations are zero; `s7_cross.out` confirms the attempted timer case. Debugger stepping returns before restarting host timers. This establishes these programs’ limitation, not a universal impossibility for every conceivable stepped guest. |
| Are the original A1/A2/D1/K1 gate dependencies real? | **TRUE.** Present in the baseline; retaining K1 would reject the fixed device. |
| Are the panel unanimity, verbatim dissent, and original premeasurement chronology independently established? | **NOT CHECKABLE from the implementation and measurement evidence reviewed.** In particular, the current PRETABLE contains a post-table-1 amendment; its present mtime cannot establish when every sentence was first written. |

The five accesses enumerate as:

| Register | Offset, decimal | Declined direction |
|---|---:|---|
| IRQ_STATUS | `0x180`, 384 | Write |
| IRQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x184`, 388 | Write |
| IRQ_ENABLE_CLEAR | `0x18c`, 396 | Read |
| FIQ_STATUS | `0x280`, 640 | Write |
| FIQ_RAW_STATUS | `0x284`, 644 | Write |

The read-only legend is indeed under PCI configuration §7.1. Applying its terminology elsewhere is an interpretation; Table 7-10 and the interrupt-controller text directly establish the directions. Zero for +0x18c remains a defensible fork policy, rather than an explicit register-specific datasheet promise.

**2. Q2 — Smallest edits that break behavior and survive**

The strongest survivor is a **single field substitution** in [IRQ_STATUS’s legal read](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/src/devices/dev_footbridge.c:572):

- Replace the `irq_status` operand with `fiq_status`: `0x50 & 0x30 = 0x10`.
- Alternatively, replace the `irq_enable` operand with `fiq_status`: `0x18 & 0x50 = 0x10`.

Both leave **P0 and P7 green**. Guest IRQ/FIQ status fields are zero, so their observations remain zero too. Neither substitution affects preservation or announcement counts. **I predict the frozen 36/36 and 111/111 results remain green. These product mutants were not executed during this review.**

The requested candidates resolve as follows:

| Candidate | Result |
|---|---|
| Restrict another site’s announcement to `len == 4` | **Survives.** P2–P5 access the word first, then test already-latched byte/halfword accesses. P9 exercises only IRQ_STATUS. |
| Exclude halfword-first announcements at IRQ_STATUS | **Survives too.** P9’s fresh access is a byte, not a halfword. |
| Clear `warned_fiq_status_w` during a legal FIQ_RAW_STATUS read | **Survives.** In S, that read occurs after both FIQ_STATUS declines. P0 reads it before latching; P7 reads it after the decline tests. No later decline checks whether the latch was rearmed. |
| Store into the other family’s status | **Caught.** P_STATE_OK checks all four status/enable fields after each site; final guest raw reads also expose persistent nonzero corruption. |
| Keep the register name but remove `(once)` | **Caught.** Every N-row’s expected substring includes `(once)`; its count becomes zero and N0 fails. |
| Substitute a duplicate green line for a missing gate row | **Accepted by several pins.** I checked the regexes against modified copies of the recorded FIX log: replacing SA1 with SC1, S2 with S1, or SF2 with SF1 preserves the corresponding counts. N1–N5 have a distinct-ID check; these groups do not. This is a gate-check weakness, not a measured product-mutant escape. |

There is another width gap: [P3’s narrow reads](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/diff_footbridge.c:675) do not score their returned bytes or handler return values. The first word read returning zero does not establish those properties for byte and halfword reads.

**3. Q3 — The floor**

**(a) Pre-fix failure: satisfied.** Independently recounted the logs: 21 green and 15 red sites rows, totaling 36; the differential stops before producing P1’s result. The two intentional comparator-selfcheck failures printed in the differential logs are subsequently unrecorded and are not detector failures.

**(b) Verbatim reinstatement at every site: supported by control flow, incompletely measured.** S reaches its first declines at program offsets `0x40`, `0x4c`, `0x58`, `0x68`, `0x74`. Reinstating the corresponding terminating branch prevents the final register capture. The offline loop likewise visits P1 through P5. Table 2 measures only the FIQ_RAW_STATUS reinstatement; four isolated reinstatement measurements remain owed.

I decoded the S memory instructions. All have `cond=14, I=0, P=1, U=1, B=0, W=0, Rn=0`; their remaining fields are:

| Program offset(s) | Word | L | Rd | imm12 |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|
| 30 | `e5808188` | 0 | 8 | 392 |
| 38 | `e5809288` | 0 | 9 | 648 |
| 40, 48 | `e5802180` | 0 | 2 | 384 |
| 44 | `e5907184` | 1 | 7 | 388 |
| 4c, 54 | `e5802184` | 0 | 2 | 388 |
| 50 | `e590a180` | 1 | 10 | 384 |
| 58 | `e590918c` | 1 | 9 | 396 |
| 5c | `e590b188` | 1 | 11 | 392 |
| 60 | `e590818c` | 1 | 8 | 396 |
| 68, 70 | `e580c280` | 0 | 12 | 640 |
| 6c | `e5902280` | 1 | 2 | 640 |
| 74, 7c | `e580c284` | 0 | 12 | 644 |
| 78 | `e590e284` | 1 | 14 | 644 |
| 80 | `e5901184` | 1 | 1 | 388 |
| 84 | `e590d284` | 1 | 13 | 644 |

Thus the disputed access directions, offsets, widths, and destination registers agree with the actual words.

**(c) Null medium: supported by the predicates; not measured in table 2.** Missing/zero device responses fail the vendor and timer-mask anchors, S3, and announcement counts. A dead or empty launcher fails fresh-register/prologue checks. Several zero-valued rows individually remain green; the composite does not.

**(d) DEPENDS-ON closure: L2 BREACH.**

The edited bodies are dominated by address selection, direction selection, and these five predicates:

- `!warned_irq_status_w`
- `!warned_irq_raw_w`
- `!warned_irq_clear_r`
- `!warned_fiq_status_w`
- `!warned_fiq_raw_w`

For the four writes, the decline follows the false branch of `writeflag == MEM_READ`; for +0x18c it follows the false branch of `writeflag == MEM_WRITE`. The tail separately tests `writeflag == MEM_READ`.

The repeated accesses and five distinct announcements distinguish latch negation/removal, wrong latch tests, and shared latch assignments. They do not establish persistence after all legal sibling reads or first access at every width.

More decisively, enumerate the read-field substitutions with `I=0x18`, `E=0x30`, `F=0x50`, `G=0xc0`:

| Expression | Replace left operand | Replace right operand |
|---|---|---|
| IRQ masked `I&E`, expected `10` | `E&E=30`; **`F&E=10` survives**; `G&E=00` | `I&I=18`; **`I&F=10` survives**; `I&G=00` |
| FIQ masked `F&G`, expected `40` | `I&G=00`; `E&G=00`; `G&G=c0` | `F&I=10`; `F&E=10`; `F&F=50` |

Raw IRQ replacements yield `30/50/c0`, against `18`; raw FIQ replacements yield `18/30/c0`, against `50`. Those are distinguished. Commuting the original AND operands is equivalent and should not count as a kill obligation.

The seed choice therefore kills dropped masks while missing two wrong-field forms explicitly within this review’s closure requirement.

**(e) Disclosure: incomplete.** The guest/nonzero-status and synthetic-FIQ limitations are disclosed. First-width coverage, narrow-read results, latch rearming, and the two operand collisions are not adequately disclosed. “Widths … rowed OFFLINE” overstates what is checked. This contributes to the L2 finding.

**(f) Relative-only rows: no separate breach found.** State checks use literal seeds; P0 uses literal expected expressions; announcement deltas have initial and total-count anchors. C4’s inequality is paired with MMU-state and positive device controls. Relative comparisons exist, but the composite has independent absolute anchors.

**4. Q4 — Harness legs**

The implemented ordering is correct in all three frozen launchers:

| Launcher | Prologue verification | Observation before teardown |
|---|---|---|
| [Sites](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/footbridge_sites_probe.py:372) | Successful stepping, no disassembly mismatches, fresh r3/r5/r6/r1 | `quit`, `reg`, capture `dead`, then teardown |
| [Drain](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/fbpending_drain_probe.py:625) | `not bad`, exactly 21 executed entries, fresh r3/r5/r6/r7 | Lines 629–632 preserve that ordering |
| [Witness](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/artifacts/fbd5/killtable_20260919/frozen/fbpending_witness.py:505) | `not bad`, exactly 18 executed entries, fresh r3/r5/r6/r7 | Lines 509–512 preserve that ordering |

The counts enumerate `0x00,04,…,50` = 21 and `0x00,04,…,44` = 18. The register comparisons require the requested four values, and the scoring requires normal exit 0.

H1/H2 establish startup rejection and clean death after useful execution. **They do not reproduce the former control’s device-triggered, mid-session exit-1 channel.** The sites HEAD/M1 runs supply separate evidence for that channel; these pending-launcher legs do not.

Sites `send()` now forwards its timeout correctly. The actual [hygiene pattern](C:/DocumentNoSnc/CC/GXEMUL/GXEMUL-SEC/regress/gate_hygiene.sh:553) accepts either a comma or closing parenthesis after the echo expression, so it recognizes both forms. No hygiene change is required; the probe’s claim that only the old literal form is recognized is stale.

A smaller vacuous H2 edit than moving teardown is to **reuse `quit_rc` as `next_rc` instead of sending the additional `reg`**. Recorded drain/witness FIX results already have `quit_rc=False`, `dead=True`, exit 0. H2 would stay green while no longer testing another ordinary interaction. This is a static prediction, not a newly executed mutant.

M13 also remains a valid concern: writing through a closed descriptor sets `dead` in the existing exception handler. “Before teardown by construction” describes today’s placement; it does not make a reordered harness self-detecting.

**5. Q5 — Option set and records**

**Member 3 remains appropriate.** The product branches preserve architectural state, provide the selected zero-read policy, retain the common tail, and bound announcements. The rejection concerns the detector floor and records.

The differential count is correct:

| Group | Rows |
|---|---:|
| P0 | 5 |
| P1 | 13 |
| P2 | 13 |
| P3 | 14 |
| P4 | 13 |
| P5 | 13 |
| P6 | 1 |
| P7 | 2 |
| P8 | 2 |
| P9 | 2 |
| **Added** | **78** |

Each write-site group contains return 1, first announcement, four preservation checks, interrupt-output check, repeated-announcement check, combined narrow-access announcement check, and four final preservation checks: **13**. P3 adds the first word-read value: **14**. Existing 33 + added 78 = **111**.

The sites rows also enumerate correctly: C1–C5 (5), A1/A2/A2b (3), I1 (1), H1/H2 (2), SC1/SA1 (2), S1–S5 (5), SF1/SF2 (2), N0–N5 (6), EC1/EA1 (2), E0–E6 (7), D1 (1): **36**.

These records need correction:

- Both pending-probe headers still describe K as a currently required terminating control.
- The sites header says FIQ expressions are “unpinned everywhere”; P0 and M8 contradict that wording. Say “not established through reachable nonzero guest state.”
- “fiq_enable … 0 throughout” is false across the new S arm, which writes `0xc0`.
- “S3 … a legal EnableClear write works, a zero write preserves” misidentifies S3. It reads IRQEnable; neither E5 nor P8 tests a zero Clear write.
- The differential comment says callbacks are NULL although they are installed before accesses.
- `0x70` is not “inside BOTH enables”: `70 & 30 = 30`, `70 & c0 = 40`. It has nonzero overlap with each.
- PRETABLE calls M13 measured; the final table explicitly says it was not built. The header’s “must red” assertion also contradicts PRETABLE’s explanation of why it greens.
- PRETABLE says SC1/SA1 cannot vary under listed mutants, but M1 makes both red.
- The working gate also contains an E-row uniqueness change absent from the frozen gate patch, beyond the separately disclosed unrelated tail.

One additional datasheet point: §7.3.30 says IRQRawStatus and FIQRawStatus always contain identical data. The `0x18/0x50` seeds violate that hardware invariant as well as current product reachability. They are legitimate synthetic detector inputs, but cannot establish hardware fidelity.

Before changing this verdict, add discriminating expression seeds, fresh byte/halfword cases for every site, returned-value/return-code checks for every declined read width, and a decline–legal-read–decline sequence covering latch persistence. Strengthen the missing distinct-ID gate pins and correct the records. Measure the additional mutants and the four remaining isolated reinstatements.

Seat record: **GPT-6 / Codex**. Read the entire brief, all six frozen diffs, relevant source and launcher paths, datasheet passages, PRETABLE/TABLE2, pass-1 `s6/s7/s8/s10` evidence, and parsed the recorded FIX/HEAD/mutant logs. Ran read-only Git, searches, hashing, in-memory patch comparisons, AST instruction decoding, arithmetic/row enumeration, and regex checks on log copies. **No emulator, compiler, gate, `run.sh`, or battery was run; no files were changed.**
